# eq-field changelog

## 2026-09-04 (PR #914 MERGED, v3.5.672 — FIX: Roster's write path had the same duplicate/spurious-failure risk as Timesheets did)
- Same-session follow-up to PR #911 below — flagged there as a known, deliberately-unaddressed gap: `_sbRosterCanon` (same file, `schedule` table) has the textually identical delete-then-insert non-atomicity that let Timesheets silently duplicate rows under a retried write-queue request.
- Checked live before assuming the risk was identical, rather than assuming symmetry: `ehow` (SKS) already carries a real unique index on `schedule_entries(staff_id, date)`, added independently of this session. A racing duplicate save there was already failing loudly (unique-violation) instead of silently duplicating — a reliability gap, not a data-integrity one. `zaap` (eq tenant) had no such index at all and was genuinely exposed to the identical silent-duplication risk Timesheets had.
- Fix: the write now upserts each day's row via `ON CONFLICT (staff_id, date)` and deletes only the specific days a save is actually clearing, instead of blindly deleting the whole week and reinserting. New migration (`20260904_schedule_entries_staff_date_unique_zaap_parity.sql`) makes the same unique index live on `zaap` too — idempotent no-op on `ehow`, closes the real gap on `zaap`. Verified zero pre-existing `(staff_id,date)` duplicate groups on either project before applying.
- `tests/supabase-canon-write.test.js`: mock fetch upgraded to enforce the same `(staff_id,date)` uniqueness on an on_conflict upsert. 2 new tests: an identical save sent twice leaves exactly one row, not two; clearing one day in a save that still carries another day's existing value only removes the cleared day's row.
- Confirmed timesheets and roster are the only two canonical write paths with this delete-then-insert shape in the codebase (`leave_requests` has no equivalent dispatch) — both are now closed.
- Rebased once to merge: pushed initially as v3.5.671, but PR #913 (roster.js file-size split, below) merged the same number first. `index.html`'s CHANGES banner conflicted for real on the shared append-point (resolved by keeping both entries, this one renumbered to v3.5.672); `app-state.js`/`supabase-canon-write.js`'s own two `?v=` tags again silently kept the stale literal with no conflict flagged — bumped by hand. One real documentation defect found mid-rebase: `docs/reflection-log.md` auto-merged in two stray lines under this PR's own entry describing a rebase-past-#911 history this branch never actually had (it was branched fresh from a base that already included #911) — removed rather than left standing.
- Verified: full suite green, `tests/supabase-canon-write.test.js` 22/22 (was 16). `build-bundles.mjs --check` clean, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean, `eslint@9` 0 errors (942 pre-existing-class warnings). Migration verified live post-apply on both projects via `pg_indexes`. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; proven instead via the two new tests reproducing a retried save and a selective clear against the real, unmodified write-path code. Royce: "merge if safe" — checked (came back `CONFLICTING` against #913), rebased, re-verified, merged once genuinely clean. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.672.

## 2026-09-04 (PR #913 MERGED, v3.5.671 — file-size convention: roster.js's My Schedule render split out)
- Direct follow-up to the file-size ratchet finding logged at PR #909's close (below): re-verified every tracked file's line count and ceiling fresh against `origin/main` before picking a target — an early read had come from a stale, 38-commits-behind shared-root checkout and briefly suggested `roster.js` wasn't tight anymore; caught and corrected before any wrong-file work happened. Confirmed `roster.js` (10 lines headroom) and `leave.js` (11, see the paired entry below) as the two genuinely tightest, matching the original finding's own pick.
- `renderRoster()`/`renderEditor()` are both bigger than `renderSchedule()` but far more central — the actual desktop/mobile roster and editor grids, deeply wired into this file's other helpers. `renderSchedule()` (My Schedule — the worker-facing single-person week view) is the biggest block that's cleanly separable on its own, so it moved to new `scripts/roster-my-schedule.js` along with `loggedJobsForDisplay()` (its only caller) rather than bumping the ceiling again. `roster.js`: 1,940 → 1,779 lines; ceiling lowered 1,950 → 1,800.
- New file added to `lazy-loader.js`'s `'roster'`/`'editor'`/`'schedule'` TAB_SCRIPTS buckets — the same footprint as `roster.js`'s other five companion files (`roster-cell-picker.js`/`roster-tools.js`/`roster-week-nav.js`/`roster-overview.js`/`roster-overview-map.js`). `tests/lazy-tab-script-guard.test.js` caught a real gap on the first attempt: placing the new file in only the `'schedule'` bucket (its semantic home) missed that `roster-week-nav.js:112` calls `renderSchedule()` from its own kind-dispatch, and that file ships in all three buckets — fixed by matching the established footprint rather than trusting manual reachability tracing. 400 passed, 0 failed after.
- Two existing tests (`tests/schedule-workbench-job.test.js`, `tests/week-window-narrowing.test.js`) read `roster.js`'s source text via regex to isolate the moved functions for testing — both broke on the move (regex found nothing, not a false pass) and were updated to read the new file instead. Same functions, unchanged behaviour.
- Rebased twice before landing: once pre-emptively (stash + fast-forward rebase + pop, no commit yet to conflict) to land on v3.5.671 as the correct next-free number after the paired leave.js PR (#912) took v3.5.670; a second time after #912 actually merged, to resolve textual conflicts on the shared version-stamp files (`app-state.js`/`sw.js`/`index.html`'s two `<script>` tags, the CHANGES banner, `docs/reflection-log.md`'s append-point) — no renumbering needed that time, v3.5.671 was already correct. Full verification loop re-run after each rebase.
- Verified: full suite green (400/400 on the guard test alone), `build-bundles.mjs --check` clean, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean, `eslint@9` 0 errors. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; proven instead via the comprehensive automated suite, including two tests updated specifically for the exact moved code paths. Royce merged both this PR and #912 explicitly ("merge #912 and #913"); confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.671.

## 2026-09-04 (PR #912 MERGED, v3.5.670 — file-size convention: leave.js's new-request flow split out)
- Same file-size ratchet finding as above (logged at PR #909's close): `leave.js` was back down to 11 lines of headroom against its 1,750-line ceiling (routine feature growth since the leave-email.js split at v3.5.554).
- The new-request flow — mode/day picking (range vs individual days), the requester + approver dropdowns, and submit (backdated-leave confirm plus the canonical-staff-resolution guards on both requester and approver) — is the next-biggest self-contained block in the file after the email templates already split out, so it moved to new `scripts/leave-submit.js` rather than bumping the ceiling again. Owns its own `leaveMode`/`pickedDays` module state, moved with it (confirmed via repo-wide grep: used nowhere else in `leave.js`, and the 8 moved functions are called only from `index.html` onclick attributes and `leave.js` itself). `leave.js`: 1,739 → 1,455 lines — clear of the plain 1,500-line default, so its `eslint.config.js` grandfather entry was removed outright rather than lowered, same call as `auth.js`'s own earlier split.
- New file joins `leave.js` in `lazy-loader.js`'s existing `'leave'` TAB_SCRIPTS bucket (`leave.js` is only ever loaded from that one bucket, so no other bucket needed updating). `tests/lazy-tab-script-guard.test.js` confirmed no cross-file reference gap was introduced (430 passed, 0 failed).
- Also fixed two cache-buster misses `check-cache-busters.mjs` caught locally before commit: `app-state.js`'s own `?v=` tag (it's eagerly `<script>`-tagged, not lazy-loaded) and `core-bundle-b1.js`, which bundles `lazy-loader.js` as one of its 3 source files.
- Rebased once to merge: pushed initially as v3.5.669, but PR #911 (Timesheets duplicate-hours fix) merged the same number first. `docs/reflection-log.md` and `index.html`'s CHANGES banner conflicted for real on the shared append-point (resolved by keeping both entries, this one renumbered to v3.5.670); `app-state.js`/`sw.js`/`index.html`'s two `<script>` tags all silently kept the stale `3.5.669` literal post-rebase with no conflict flagged (git sees identical text on both sides and doesn't consider that a conflict) — caught by hand, same trap this changelog's other 2026-09-04 entries already document.
- Verified: full suite green, `build-bundles.mjs --check` clean, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean, `eslint@9` 0 errors (939 pre-existing warnings, same baseline as main). Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation. Royce merged both this PR and #913 explicitly ("merge #912 and #913"); confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.671 (the later of the two, built on top of this one).

## 2026-09-04 (PR #911 MERGED, v3.5.669 — FIX: Timesheets could still duplicate a day's hours from completely normal single-cell editing)
- Direct continuation of PR #906 (below), same day: Cihan Alakuzu and Maylin Ung showed wildly inflated hours again, 25 minutes after #906 had already shipped — this time confirmed live via the audit log to be completely normal, one-cell-at-a-time editing, not a client-side loop.
- Root cause was NOT the same as #906 — that fix closed client-side loops calling `saveTsCell` once per day in one action. This time the underlying write path itself (`_sbTimesheetsCanon`, `supabase-canon-write.js`) replaced a person's whole week via a DELETE then a separate INSERT — two non-atomic requests. A slow response the app's own write-queue retries fires an independent copy of that same pair; if the original also eventually lands, both inserts survive as duplicates. Up to 19 duplicate copies of a single day confirmed live.
- Fix: the write now upserts each day/job row via `ON CONFLICT (staff_id, date, task)` — new unique constraint (`UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, migration `20260904_timesheets_staff_date_task_unique.sql`) applied live to both `ehowgjardagevnrluult` and `zaapmfdkgedqupfjtchl`, zero pre-existing violations on either — and deletes only the specific rows a save is actually clearing.
- Cihan's and Maylin's current-week duplicate rows cleaned up directly (Royce's explicit approval), reconstructed from each day's most recent save: Maylin 46.5h, Cihan 81.5h.
- `tests/timesheets-canon-write.test.js` and `tests/timesheets-bulk-flush-regression.test.js`: mock fetch upgraded to actually enforce the new `(staff_id,date,task)` uniqueness on an on_conflict upsert — neither file could otherwise prove anything about the fix. New tests reproduce the exact live incident (an identical save sent twice must leave exactly one row) and confirm a genuinely cleared day is deleted without touching a different day's row.
- Known gap, deliberately not fixed in this PR: `_sbRosterCanon` (same file, the `schedule` table's write path) has the identical delete-then-insert architecture and is equally exposed to the same write-queue-retry risk — closed separately, see PR #914 above.
- Verified: full suite green, `tests/timesheets-canon-write.test.js` 15/15 (was 5), `tests/timesheets-bulk-flush-regression.test.js` 4/4. `build-bundles.mjs --check` clean, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean, `eslint@9` 0 errors (940 pre-existing-class warnings). Migration verified live post-apply on both projects via `pg_get_constraintdef`. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; proven instead via the two updated test files. Royce: "merge if safe" — checked (came back `CONFLICTING` against a same-day PR that had just landed), rebased, renumbered, re-verified, merged once genuinely clean. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.669.

## 2026-09-04 (PR #909 MERGED, v3.5.667 — Timesheets: TAFE/Leave autofill + dark-cell tooltip)
- Same-day follow-up to PR #906 below: Royce asked to revisit the dark-cell question and separately asked what's built between TAFE/holiday and Timesheets, wanting an autofill for known TAFE/leave days that stays editable.
- Dark cell: `sp-work-cont` continuation shade now carries a tooltip naming the job it's continuing — styling itself was already correct (see PR #906's Thursday-dark answer below), it just had no explanation.
- TAFE/Leave autofill: `dayStatus()` (`timesheets-rules.js`) already detected a nominated TAFE day or approved leave and silently credited a virtual 8h to the weekly total, but nothing was ever written to the real row — invisible to exports, and the grid showed a fully locked chip with no override. Put the automatic-vs-one-tap design fork to Royce via `AskUserQuestion` before writing code; he picked one-tap, matching every other fill action in the app. `prefillTsFromRoster()` and `_computeMyWeekPrefill()` now also fill TAFE/approved-leave days with a real, editable 8h entry (same label the chip shows); the spans grid's TAFE/Leave chips are now clickable (supervisor only), opening the same day editor as any other cell.
- Found and fixed in passing: a configured "TAFE Holidays" break wasn't actually being checked by `dayStatus()` — already-logged gap (2026-09-02 apprentices entry), closed here since it now matters more (a holiday-week false-positive can produce a real written row, not just a display total). 3 new tests cover the holiday-override behaviour and confirm the explicit roster-typed TAFE code path is untouched.
- Merge required a rebase: `main` had moved to v3.5.666 via concurrent PR #908 (below), which this branch's own v3.5.666 collided with. `docs/reflection-log.md`, `eslint.config.js`'s ratchet comment, and `index.html`'s CHANGES banner conflicted for real on the shared append-point (resolved keeping both PRs' content); `scripts/timesheets.js` auto-merged cleanly (different functions touched); `app-state.js`/`sw.js`/index.html's two `<script>` version tags all silently kept the stale `3.5.666` literal with no conflict flagged (same trap as every other PR today) — caught by hand, renumbered to v3.5.667. Combined, the two PRs pushed `timesheets.js` to 2,527 lines past its same-day ceiling bump to 2500 — bumped again to 2550.
- Verified: full suite 41/41 green (38 pre-existing + 3 new holiday-override cases), `build-bundles.mjs --check` clean, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean, `eslint@9` 0 errors/939 pre-existing-class warnings post-rebase. Re-checked `mergeable: MERGEABLE`/`mergeStateStatus: CLEAN` and no further `main` drift immediately before merging. Merged on Royce's "merge if safe," confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.667. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; UI-glue paths (clickable chips, new prefill branches) verified by manual trace + full suite only, the rules-layer fix has real test coverage.
- Session-close file-size review (Royce asked directly): every tracked large file in this repo (`timesheets.js`, `roster.js`, `leave.js`, `sks-pipeline.js`, `sks-pipeline-resource.js`, `tender-pipeline.js`) is now within 0.5%–2% of its `eslint.config.js` ratchet ceiling, not just `timesheets.js` — logged as an open decision in `eq/pending/eq-field.md` (ratchet-as-it-comes-up vs. a real decomposition pass) rather than resolved unilaterally.

## 2026-09-04 (PR #906 MERGED, v3.5.664 — FIX: Timesheets duplicate-hours bug + scroll-jump-to-top)
- Royce: David Boyd struggling to enter timesheets, Cihan Alakuzu showing 120h for a normal week, and a screenshot asking why one day's cell renders darker than its neighbours. Investigated all three against live ehow data and the actual render code, not guessed.
- Root cause of the 120h: `saveTsCell()` always reposts the whole week's row (by design, for the legacy wide table), but on SKS's canonical write path each repost fans out into a fresh per-day INSERT rather than an upsert. Eight call sites (Fill week, Copy last week, DNW, Repeat day, Prefill from roster, CSV import, and the +Add job editor's Save/Clear days) looped `saveTsCell` once per day — day 1 got reposted on every later iteration, compounding into duplicate rows. Confirmed live: Cihan's job 27862 had 5/4/3/2/1 duplicate rows across Mon–Fri, summing to exactly 120h.
- Fix: `saveTsCell` split into an in-memory update (`_tsSetEntryDay`) and a single network flush (`_tsFlushRow`); new `saveTsCellsBulk()` lets every multi-day caller update all its days in memory and hit the network exactly once. A clean single-threaded Node repro against the real, unmodified `_sbTimesheetsCanon` write path did NOT reproduce the duplication when properly sequenced — so this closes the confirmed trigger (redundant reposts), not a claimed root cause of the DB-level mechanism itself. A table-wide scan found this was the only duplicate anywhere in `app_data.field_timesheets`'s history, consistent with rapid repeated calls being the real trigger rather than a permanently-broken delete.
- Royce approved cleaning up Cihan's existing 14 duplicate rows in the same session — deleted directly against ehow, kept one correct 8h/job-27862 row per date, leaving 40h.
- Scroll jump: `renderTimesheets()`'s scroll-preserve logic read/wrote `#page-timesheets`'s own `scrollTop`, but the real `overflow:auto` element is the child `.table-scroll` — `#page-timesheets` itself never scrolls, so the "restore" was always a no-op. Fixed at the source so every full-table rebuild (every bulk action above, plus the +Add job editor's Save) keeps its scroll position; dropped three redundant, already-broken `window.scrollY`/stale-class-name copies of the same guard.
- Thursday-dark question: not a bug — `timesheets-spans.js` deliberately darkens the 2nd+ consecutive day of the same job number to show a multi-day span. No code change.
- David Boyd's own stored data checked clean (no duplicates) — whatever he hit was most likely one of two already-fixed Sentry errors (`EQ-FIELD-1C`/`EQ-FIELD-1D`, both shipped in v3.5.642), not data loss.
- Rebased twice mid-session past two concurrent same-day PRs (#904 Prestart/Toolbox Reopen, #905 Unlock Week confirm() fix) each independently claiming this branch's version number — renumbered v3.5.662 → 663 → 664, re-verifying `app-state.js`/`sw.js` by hand both times (both silently kept the prior literal version string post-rebase with no conflict flagged, same trap those PRs' own entries already documented).
- Verified: full suite 41/41 green (new `tests/timesheets-bulk-flush-regression.test.js`), `build-bundles.mjs --check`/`check-cache-busters.mjs`/`eslint@9` all clean. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; used a Node harness against the real production write-path code instead. Merged on Royce's explicit "clean up Cihan's duplicate rows and merge it," confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.664.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #899 MERGED, v3.5.657 — supabase.js decomposition: extract the JWT-carrier)
- `scripts/supabase.js` was back at its file-size ratchet ceiling — PR #898 (merged as v3.5.656 minutes earlier) had bumped `GRANDFATHERED_MAX_LINES` 1550→1600 to land an 11-line retry fix, after the file had already been sitting at 1549/1550, and explicitly flagged the JWT-carrier (`_mintDataJwt`/`_getDataJwt`/`_getDataActorId`) as the clean extraction candidate, deferred only because of one real coupling point (`_actorIdEverSeen`, sbFetch's own write-path audit-attribution flag from the 2026-08-26 Mark Brame incident). This PR is that follow-up.
- Moved the JWT-carrier verbatim into new file `scripts/supabase-jwt-carrier.js` (must load before `supabase.js`, same classic-`<script>`-shared-scope mechanism `supabase-queue-crypto.js` already uses). `_actorIdEverSeen`/`_actorDropReported` stay declared in `supabase.js` exactly where they were; the new file writes `_actorIdEverSeen` as a bare cross-file global, same mechanism this codebase already uses everywhere else — deliberately not restructured for zero functional benefit on security-adjacent code. `supabase.js`: 1,549 → 1,479 lines; ceiling kept at 1,600 for real headroom, not lowered to the 1,500 default.
- PR #898 merged mid-task (touching the exact function being extracted) — rebased onto it and ported its retry fix into the new file rather than losing it.
- `tests/lazy-tab-script-guard.test.js` caught a real bug on the first full-suite run: the new file had no `<script>` tag in `index.html` yet, so it would never have loaded in a real browser — JWT minting would have silently broken for both tenants. Fixed before going further. Also bridged `tests/mint-data-jwt-retry.test.js`, `tests/leave-paging.test.js`, `tests/timesheets-paging.test.js` (all three `require()` `supabase.js` directly) — no test assertions changed.
- Verified: full suite 38/38 test files green, `npx eslint@9 scripts tests netlify` 0 errors (937 pre-existing warnings, same class as the rest of the codebase), cache-buster + bundle-drift checks clean. Merged on Royce's explicit "merge it" the following session; re-verified PR was still clean/CI-green before merging rather than assuming it still held a day later. Confirmed live: Netlify deploy record for the merge commit shows `state: ready`/`published_at` set (17s after merge — no build step in this repo), independently confirmed via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only sandbox limitation; no user-visible surface either way.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #889 MERGED, v3.5.649 — PERF: Shell→Field boot, entitlements/timeout fix)
- Royce, on SKS go-live day: "it logs in via shell, then field then it goes back to the core spinner... can we improve this?" Traced the full Shell→Field handoff live in code: Shell's own "Loading EQ Field…" overlay sits on top of the iframe until Field posts `'accepted'` back — that reappearing IS the "back to spinner" experience, by design (a two-stage loading UI), not a reload bug.
- Found one confirmed inefficiency: `_loadCanonicalConfig()` (`scripts/app-state.js`) awaited the canonical `module_entitlements` fetch before returning, delaying `window._eqOrgSlugReady()` — the signal that unblocks BOTH `checkAccess()`/verify-pin (the call that actually clears Shell's overlay) and the tenant-routing fetch — even though entitlements isn't consumed until after both. Now returns as soon as org resolves, hands back entitlements as a still-in-flight promise, awaited later at first real use.
- `netlify/functions/tenant-config.js`'s two upstream fetches (canonical routing lookup, tenant-DB org_id enrichment) were both unbounded. Live Sentry evidence the same day (EQ-FIELD-19, AbortError at `loadTenantConfig-routing-fetch`, real SKS tenant, several occurrences, regressed) showed this consuming the client's entire 4s/7s retry budget. Both now bounded (2.5s/1s — the second is pure enrichment with an existing client fallback, cut tight).
- Version renumbered 3.5.648→3.5.649 mid-session: PR #888 (unrelated Edit Roster fix) merged claiming v3.5.648 while this branch was in progress — rebased and renumbered, same collision class already tracked.
- This investigation continued into eq-shell once Sentry showed the bigger number (Shell's own `token-exchange`, 2.65s avg) lived there — see `eq/changelog/eq-shell.md`, three more PRs (#1747/#1749/#1750) same date.
- `node tests/*.test.js` full suite green, `eslint` 0 new errors, `check-cache-busters.mjs` clean. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing v3.5.649.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #892 MERGED, v3.5.651 — Apprentices: journal sharing replaces Ask-for-Feedback, one loop instead of two)
- Same session as the Quarterly Review fix below. Full-module audit (2 parallel agents + a live usage check: only 3 apprentice profiles ever created, all in the 4 days before this session; 5 of 6 activity tables at zero rows) found the module had barely been used. Royce read the audit, said it feels complicated, and described what he wants: apprentices able to note things down at their leisure, trusting he'll read and act on it, plus accountability tracking.
- `apprentice_journal` gains `acknowledged_at`/`acknowledged_by`/`acknowledged_note` (additive migration, applied live to ehow). New `journalAcknowledge()` mirrors `resolveFollowUp()`'s one-tap-with-optional-note pattern. Two new surfaces (per-profile Overview card next to Follow-ups; list-level inbox card across every apprentice) replace two removed ones (Pending Requests card, Inbound Asks card).
- Retired: the apprentice-initiated Ask-for-Feedback flow (named-supervisor picker, prompt, email with a `?request=<uuid>` deep link) — client forms, the `request-feedback` server action, the `feedback_requests` read, the modal. `feedback_requests` table left in place (RLS/grants intact, 0 rows written in ~2 months live) rather than dropped.
- Added 3 PostHog events (`journal_entry_created`/`_shared`/`_acknowledged`) — Royce: "initial goal is to see if this helps them engage... final outcome would be to hopefully have better people in our business." Closes the "no usage instrumentation" gap from the 2026-08-30 Apprentices critique.
- Royce's explicit call, not built: no notification when an entry is shared — "happy if it's a closed loop between me and apprentices... right now." `apprentice_profiles.supervisor_id` could route one later without reintroducing the old "pick a person" step.
- Audit also surfaced, not fixed this PR: `addCustomCompetency`/`removeCustomCompetency` bypass the ownership-checked write gateway (security-adjacent); the per-person `canManageApprentices()` permission migration reached only 1 of 4 remaining files; TAFE Holidays doesn't protect Timesheets' auto-prefill, only Roster's bulk-fill button; a duplicate/dead `_isSelfProfile()` definition; an unreachable Skills Passport render optimization. Full detail: `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.
- Two same-day version collisions rebased through (v3.5.650 claimed by a concurrent PR first; renumbered to v3.5.651). `build-bundles.mjs --check` caught `core-bundle-a1.js` drift (bundles `analytics.js` as a source) before it shipped stale.
- Verified: full test suite green throughout (37/37 files), cache-buster + bundle-drift checks clean, live grep swept the whole `scripts/` tree for every retired symbol. Not click-tested live — Royce is testing this one himself, live, from an apprentice's phone.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #887 MERGED, v3.5.647 — FIX: Apprentices Quarterly Review modal unstyled and wouldn't close)
- Royce shared a screenshot of the "+ New Review" form looking broken — a gray-navy wash, raw unstyled fields. Root cause: `class="modal-box"`/`class="input"` don't exist anywhere in the stylesheet (real classes: `.modal`/`.form-input`), live since the feature shipped (v3.5.168), untouched through a later file-size split.
- Second bug in the same function: opened via `style.display='flex'` instead of the shared `openModal()`, so the shared `closeModal()` (wired to Cancel and to Save) could never actually close it — it only ever removes the `.open` class, never touches inline style.
- Verified the fix with `getComputedStyle()` against the real stylesheet (before: transparent background, `font-family: monospace`; after: white card, `Plus Jakarta Sans` throughout) rather than guessing. Checked live `quarterly_reviews` for duplicate saves the stuck-modal might have caused — none found (table was, and still is until real usage starts, empty).
- Full test suite green, cache-buster/bundle checks clean. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only limitation.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #891 MERGED, v3.5.650 — Batch Fill: never write over someone's approved leave)
- Same root cause as PR #888 below, at bulk scale: leave never gets written into the raw schedule cell, so Batch Fill's picker and its own pre-existing "already has a value?" conflict check both had nothing to see.
- Checked real usage before proposing a fix: `public.audit_log` shows 4 Batch Fill/Clear operations by Royce in the last month, 20–400 cells each — not rare. Cross-referencing those weeks against live `leave_requests`/`schedule_entries` found this had already happened: 4 people (Brett Kilpatrick, Marcus De La Fuente, Elliot Gross, Blake Reynolds) currently carry a real site code on a day they also hold approved RDO/Annual leave — live on `ehow` for this coming Friday (2026-09-04), not hypothetical.
- Fix: `buildBatchPeopleList()` shows an "On leave" badge per person in the picker. `runBatchFill()`'s existing conflict-detection now also catches leave days and folds them into the same confirm dialog as information, not a choice. `applyBatch()` enforces the skip unconditionally for a fill — no override path in the UI, deliberately stricter than Edit Roster's tint-but-allow, since a bulk operation gives nobody per-cell visibility to catch a mistake themselves. A clear stays exempt — removing a real value off someone's leave day is the fix for a conflict, not a new risk.
- Verified via a headless Node `vm` sandbox running the real `batch.js`+`roster.js`+`roster-rules.js` source against data shaped like the live incident — 4 scenarios, all matched expectations. Full suite green (37/37).
- **Merged later the same day** (not by this session — picked up while rebasing the Apprentices PR above past it). Not click-tested live by a person as of this write.
- Separately, not part of this PR: the 4 live mis-scheduled people above were flagged directly to Royce — needs a human check before Friday, not a code or DB fix.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #888 MERGED, v3.5.648 — Edit Roster now shows approved leave while editing)
- Follow-on from PR #886 below. Royce found Jessica Robinson's Friday cell on Edit Roster read as blank despite her genuine approved leave (2026-10-23→30) — typed a real site code onto it, then had to clear it once he learned she was on leave, and asked why Calendar/Weekly Roster kept showing the leave regardless.
- Root cause: Edit Roster deliberately shows the raw schedule cell (`scripts/roster.js` `renderEditor()`), not the leave overlay Weekly Roster/Calendar use, so supervisors can actually see/edit what's stored. But leave hasn't been written into schedule cells since v3.5.281 (`leave_requests` is the single source of truth) — so the page's own "needs filling in" empty-cell check (`isOnLeaveAllWeek`, which tested those same raw cells) had been structurally dead ever since that architecture change, and a leave day got flagged exactly like a genuinely unrostered one.
- Fix: `renderEditor()` now reuses `approvedLeaveCode()` — the same function Weekly Roster/Calendar already call — to suppress the false empty-cell nag and show a distinct, softer amber `.leave-cell` tint + the leave code as placeholder + a tooltip. Deliberately still visible even if a real value already sits on that day (the conflict case), so the signal doesn't disappear the moment someone types over it.
- Verified via a headless Node `vm` sandbox running the real `roster.js`+`roster-rules.js` source against live-shaped data (Jessica's leave-covered days get the tint, her genuinely-unrostered pre-leave days still get the empty-cell nag, an unrelated person's real booking is untouched, a "conflict" cell keeps both signals with no double-classing). Full suite green (37/37).
- Same investigation surfaced a 4th surface with the identical blind spot — **Batch Fill** (`scripts/batch.js` `applyBatch()`) has zero leave-awareness in its picker or its "skip existing" guard, arguably higher risk since it's a bulk write. Confirmed via direct code read; Royce: "enough" — not building this now, tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge #888" (`ef0ff0b1`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` — instruction given shortly after this session's initial `/close`, merged as a same-session follow-up rather than deferred.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #886 MERGED, v3.5.646 — Team-filtered Roster/Editor/Contacts/Timesheets show a scoped-view banner)
- Royce: "jessica robsinson leave shows on the calendar but not in the roster (October)." Verified live against ehow before touching code: Jessica Robinson is active, on-roster, a genuine member of `app_data.team_members` ("CT Team"), with a real approved leave request 2026-10-23→30.
- Root cause: not a data bug. Roster/Editor/Contacts/Timesheets share a Team-pill filter (`scripts/teams.js`, `STATE.teamFilters`) that silently hides anyone outside the selected team(s) — auto-seeded to a non-manager supervisor's own crew on every load (`_seedDefaultTeamFilter`), or a sticky explicit pick persisted indefinitely via `localStorage` (`_persistTeamFilter`) otherwise — with only a highlighted pill as the on-screen cue. Calendar applies no such filter at all (`calendar.js`'s `calPeople` reads raw `STATE.people`), so it always shows everyone regardless.
- Fix: `renderTeamPills()` now shows a banner — "Showing `<team>` only — N people hidden · View All" — whenever the active filter is hiding anyone, on all four pages that share the pill row. No visibility-rule change, purely a "you're not seeing everyone" signal.
- Verified via a headless Node `vm` sandbox running the real `teams.js` source across 4 scenarios (filter excludes her / filter includes her team / no filter / View All clears it) — all matched expectations exactly.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge it" (`0e4326ce`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`. `gh pr merge --delete-branch`'s local step failed (another concurrent worktree already had `main` checked out) even though the GitHub-side merge succeeded — confirmed via `gh pr view --json state,mergedAt,mergeCommit` rather than trusting the error, then finished the incomplete step (`git push origin --delete <branch>`) by hand.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #882 MERGED, v3.5.642 — Calendar never showed weekend roster entries)
- Royce, live example: "CA1 is logged in for 7th November for Richard Simon and Brian but it doesnt show on the calendar." Confirmed live against ehow before touching code: Richard Brown, Simon Bramall, and Brian Griffin-Colls all have a real `app_data.field_schedule` row for CA1 (Equinix CA1) on Sat 7 Nov 2026 — correctly showing on the Roster tab, absent from Calendar.
- Root cause: `renderCalendar()`'s per-day data-build loop (`scripts/calendar.js`) hard-skipped Saturday/Sunday entirely (`if (dow >= 5) continue`), so a real weekend roster entry never reached `calDayData` at all — not a Roster/Calendar data-source mismatch, both read the same `STATE.schedule`. That silently blanked the grid cell, the mobile agenda card, and the day-detail panel (`openCalPanel`, which bails on a missing `calDayData[ds]`) for every weekend, tenant-wide, not just this date.
- Fix: every day of the month now builds and renders the same way, weekday or weekend. Weekend cells keep their muted grey background/day-number styling as a visual cue, but are now clickable and show real allocations/leave when present, same as any weekday.
- Deliberate trade-off, named not softened: before this fix an empty weekend read as a flat, inert "day off" cell with no click target. After, an ordinary non-worked Saturday/Sunday is clickable and opens the panel to say "No allocations," same as an empty weekday — the simpler one-rendering-path choice over a "stay muted unless populated" special case.
- Same session also investigated (no bug found, see below) whether approved leave reaches the Weekly Roster correctly — separate report, separate root cause, bundled in one close.
- Squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`. `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`'s local branch-switch step failed (another concurrent worktree already had `main` checked out) even though the GitHub-side merge succeeded — confirmed via `gh pr view --json state,mergedAt,mergeCommit` rather than trusting the error, then finished the incomplete step (`git push origin --delete <branch>`) by hand.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #877+#878 MERGED, v3.5.638/640 — My Schedule: stale-cache "Check again" fix, day-card contacts collapsed behind a tap)
- Royce, screenshot of My Schedule: (1) logged in as Jordan Sample, "uploaded both white card and photo id and its still asking me for it," and (2) "the daily info is a little bit busy."
- **PR #877 (v3.5.638)**: verified live against canonical (jvkn) before touching code — Jordan's data was actually correct (held both required credential types); the screenshot itself showed a different person (Liam Holmgreen), whose gap was real. Root cause: `_myComplianceCard()`'s two backing caches fetch once per page load and never invalidate, so an already-open Field tab stays on stale data (from before an EQ Cards upload) with no way to recover short of a hard reload. Added a "Check again" action to the card's warning state that resets the caches and re-fetches.

My Schedule only; `sites.js`'s Sites page keeps these always open. Picked over 3 other offered directions (lighten styling only / collapse crew instead / leave as-is) via `AskUserQuestion`.
- Real version collision on #878: a concurrent PR (#879) merged as v3.5.639 (this branch's original number) moments before #878's own squash-merge attempt, which failed with a genuine GraphQL conflict. Rebased; also caught a *silent* duplicate-version auto-merge on `scripts/app-state.js`/`sw.js` (both sides had independently changed the version line to the textually identical `3.5.639`, so git resolved it with no conflict marker despite being two different changes) — re-bumped to v3.5.640 by hand. Also found and fixed a real `docs/reflection-log.md` merge corruption from #877's own earlier rebase (one entry's closing line had been misattributed to a different entry during git's 3-way auto-merge, with no conflict raised either time).
- **Note on a false "fabricated incident" claim**: a later, concurrent session (PR #880) checked `scripts/people-required-credentials.js` for "unfixed pre-fix code" and concluded #878's account of the above (version collision + reflection-log fix) was invented. That check ran against the wrong file — #878 never touched `people-required-credentials.js`, only `roster.js`. Independently re-verified against a fresh clone of `github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field`: commit `0e7d3956` (#878) is genuine and its account is accurate. Corrected in `sessions/2026-09-02.md`, `eq/pending/eq-field.md`, and eq-field memory `project_fabricated_history_pr878.md` (retracted same-day). Told Royce directly.
- Both squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.

Fixed both alongside the approved 6.

## 2026-09-01 (PR #857 MERGED, no version bump — tooling: local pre-push guard for cache-buster drift)
- The cache-buster-tag CI drift (a changed `scripts/*.js`/`styles/*.css` file whose own `?v=` tag in `index.html` didn't move) had been missed 6 times, twice more this same day (PRs #854/#856) despite the fix being documented in memory and applied once already earlier the same session — spawned `task_7499b5e4` to close the gap mechanically instead of relying on remembering to run the check.
- Built `.githooks/pre-push`: fetches `origin/main`, runs `scripts/check-cache-busters.mjs --base origin/main` (the same script `cache-buster-drift.yml` runs in CI), blocks the push on drift. Escape hatch `EQ_SKIP_CACHE_BUSTER_CHECK=1 git push`, matching the existing `EQ_SKIP_BRIEF`/`EQ_SKIP_REFLECT` convention.
- The task's framing assumed this could "mirror" an existing `.githooks`/`guard.js` already in the repo — that didn't exist. Verified `git config core.hooksPath` blank at every scope and zero tracked `guard.js`/`hooksPath` hits repo-wide first: eq-field's brief-gate/reflection-gate are global, per-machine Claude Code hooks (`~/.claude/hooks/guard.js`), not git-native, not inherited by a fresh clone. Built a real git-native hook instead.
- Activation is `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, confirmed live to be one command per *clone* (stored in the shared common `.git/config`), not per worktree — setting it once was immediately active for the shared root and every existing sibling worktree.
- Verified three ways directly (clean pass, forced-drift block, bypass flag), then confirmed the hook fires through real git plumbing on the actual `git push` of the branch itself.
- eq-field [PR #857](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/857), squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge it". No user-visible surface — nothing served to `field.eq.solutions` changed.

- Root cause — `getAllSiteCodes()` is defined in the lazy-loaded `roster.js`; `refreshPersonSelects()` (which populates the site select) is never re-triggered when the Editor tab is first opened, so a select populated before `roster.js` finished loading stayed stuck on "All Sites" with no self-heal in sight for that page.

- eq-field [PR #863](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/863), squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (v3.5.629) within seconds of merge.

One field doing double duty (name vs. site, no indication of which reason matched) is a real precision loss on a fleet with many `SY`-prefixed codes — flagged as the likely-intended tradeoff, not silently hardened further. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-31 (PR #849 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.620 — Feature Toggles: descriptions get concrete examples + a live mini-preview of what each switch actually renders)
- Royce sent a screenshot of Manage → Feature Toggles (SKS tenant) with two asks: "who can see this menu?" and "can we help with improving the descriptions / show examples of what each feature does?"
- Access question answered from live code before touching anything: `field.manage_feature_toggles` (`permission-matrix.js`) is granted to `manager` and `supervisor` only, enforced three times over — the nav item is hidden via inline style unless `canManageFeatureToggles()` is true (`auth.js`), the `PAGE_ACCESS` route guard refuses navigation otherwise (`index.html`), and the page's own render function re-checks and shows "You don't have access to this page" if somehow reached (`feature-toggles.js`).
- Scoped the description-improvement ask via `AskUserQuestion` (text-only example vs. text + visual mini-preview vs. adjust wording first) rather than guessing how far to take it — Royce picked text + visual mini-preview.
- Every example grounded in real code/data, not invented: the credential gate's example quotes its actual confirm-modal wording verbatim (`people-required-credentials.js`); "White Card" was confirmed as a real example value this app's own copy already uses elsewhere for this exact feature; SLDC/MOD10 was confirmed as the one real fleet site carrying 2+ project codes today rather than naming a fabricated site.
- FIX/FEATURE — each of the 3 toggles (`scripts/feature-toggles.js`) gets a one-line concrete example plus a small static visual mock of the real control, styled from the actual production markup (`roster.js`'s `editor-job`/`editor-project` `<select>`, `index.html`'s `#modal-confirm`) rather than an approximation of it.
- Caught and fixed a real bug in the new code before shipping: the mocks sit inside the row's `<label>` (the existing big-click-target pattern that lets clicking the description text also toggle the checkbox), so a click anywhere inside a mock would otherwise bubble to the label's default action and silently flip the real, org-wide switch. Added `event.preventDefault()` on each mock's wrapper — logic-free, just suppresses the label forwarding.
- Verified functionally, not just visually: no live Field/Core session available in this environment (both tenants are Core-only), so built an isolated harness that loads the real shipped `feature-toggles.js` file and drove it in the Browser pane — confirmed clicking inside a mock leaves the checkbox untouched (a live change-event counter stayed at 0) while clicking the row's own text or the checkbox still toggles it normally (counter incremented).
- Hit the same version-collision pattern as PR #848 below, twice, back to back: bumped to v3.5.618 (PR #847 took it first) → rebased, re-picked v3.5.619 (PR #848 below took it next, mid-flight) → rebased again, re-picked v3.5.620, re-ran the full local suite each time, re-checked mergeable state immediately before the actual merge call.
- eq-field [PR #849](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/849), squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- **Open follow-ups**: not click-tested live by a person (no Core/Shell session in this environment); and `field.manage_feature_toggles` is manager+supervisor by default even though the switches themselves are org-wide — Royce's call whether to narrow to manager-only. Both tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-31 (PR #848 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.619 — FIX: a removed person's historical leave/timesheet rows read "(unknown)" forever)
- Royce screenshotted a leave-screen warning: "Leave row references staff_id ... not found in the staff list — showing as (unknown)." Root-caused live before touching code: the `staff_id` belongs to Liam Brook-Jackson (SKS), removed from Field 2026-08-26 (`archived=true`, `active=false`, now in `app_data.field_people_removed`), whose approved July-2026 leave request still references it. `staffIdToName()` (`scripts/leave-adapter.js`, shared by the roster and timesheets adapters) only ever resolved against the LIVE staff map (`field_approved=true`), so once someone is removed, every historical row referencing them can never resolve a name again — permanently "(unknown)", even though the person's record still exists.
- Scope-checked before fixing: not isolated to the one reported row — 35 timesheet rows tenant-wide have the same orphaned-`staff_id` gap. Silent there by design (timesheets' `note()` sink is console-only to avoid toast spam, per v3.5.610's fix); only leave's `warn()` toasts to `leave.approve` holders, which is why only this one row surfaced to Royce.
- FIX — `staffIdToName()` now falls back to a removed-staff map (`setRemovedStaffRows()`, new) when the live map misses. Fallback only, one direction: never feeds `nameToStaffId()`, so a removed person still can't be picked for a *new* leave/roster/timesheet entry, and the live map always wins if a `staff_id` is ever in both.
- `loadCanonicalStaffMap()` (`scripts/supabase.js`) fetches `people_removed` — the same already-routed, already-proven-safe table `people.js`'s "Show removed" toggle reads, no new fetch pattern introduced — and wires it into the fallback. Gated to `canManagePeople()` so removed-staff PII isn't pulled into every worker's session, only a supervisor's. Non-fatal on failure, same pattern as the adjacent site-map fetch.
- `tests/leave-adapter.test.js` — 3 new assertions: fallback resolves the real name, no warning fires when it resolves, live map takes precedence over the fallback if a `staff_id` is ever in both.
- Mid-session version collision, caught before shipping: bumped to v3.5.618 (free at the time), then PR #847 landed that exact number while this PR was still open. Committed on the old base, rebased onto the new `origin/main` (one conflict in `index.html`'s changelog banner, both entries kept), re-picked v3.5.619, re-verified zero residual `3.5.618` references anywhere before pushing.
- eq-field [PR #848](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/848), squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge it", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` within ~10s of merge.
- **Open follow-up**: not click-tested live — no Field session/credentials in this environment. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #840 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.612 — FIX: Map tiles blank after v3.5.608 — CSP img-src never got the new OpenStreetMap domain)
- v3.5.608 swapped the Map / Roster Overview tile provider from CARTO to OpenStreetMap (CARTO started requiring an API key), but never updated CSP's `img-src` to allow the new domain — an oversight in that fix, not a second provider-side failure. Every tile request had been silently blocked by the browser since that release: site marker pins rendered correctly, the map background stayed completely blank, no visible error anywhere.
- Caught live: Royce's follow-up screenshot showed the CARTO watermark gone (confirming v3.5.608 itself deployed correctly, and the OpenStreetMap attribution was visibly rendering) but the tiles themselves blank. Checked the live CSP directly and confirmed `img-src` still only listed `*.basemaps.cartocdn.com`.
- FIX — `netlify.toml` and `_headers`' `img-src` directive: swapped `https://*.basemaps.cartocdn.com` for `https://*.tile.openstreetmap.org` in both files, per this repo's own standing rule that CSP must be updated in both places.
- eq-field [PR #840](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/840), squash-merged on explicit "merge", confirmed live two ways: `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` AND a direct `curl -I` of the production response, showing `img-src` now carries `tile.openstreetmap.org` with zero remaining `cartocdn` reference — a config-only fix like this is more directly verifiable via the real served header than any click-through.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #836 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.611 — Dashboard + Trial Dashboard: an aliased project code now shows its real site name too)
- Follow-up to PR #833 (v3.5.606): that PR fixed `roster.js`'s own site-name/address/job-number lookups via a new `_resolveSiteAbbr()` helper, but deliberately left `dashboard.js`/`core-bundle-b1.js` and `trial-dashboard.js` alone — each keeps its own separate, underscore-prefixed site-name fallback, used only while `roster.js` hasn't lazy-loaded yet.
- Confirmed this isn't a narrow race before fixing it: `dashboard.js` ships eagerly inside `core-bundle-b1.js` on every boot, but `roster.js` is lazy per-tab and isn't a declared dependency of the `'dashboard'` or `'trial'` lazy-loader tab entries — a session that never opens Roster sits on the unresolved fallback the whole time.
- FIX — `scripts/dashboard.js`: `_getSiteName`'s fallback now mirrors `_resolveSiteAbbr` (direct `STATE.sites` match → `EQ_ROSTER_ADAPTER.codeToSiteOrProject`/`siteIdToCode` alias lookup → give up), instead of a plain abbr match with no alias path.
- FIX — `scripts/trial-dashboard.js`: its equivalent fallback was even thinner (raw code, no `STATE.sites` lookup at all) — brought to the same behaviour.
- `scripts/core-bundle-b1.js` regenerated via `build-bundles.mjs --write` (dashboard.js is a bundle source). `site-map.js` (Dashboard's map view) checked — no site-abbr lookup of its own, nothing to change.
- Live-verified in the deploy preview (browser, `?tenant=demo`, no login needed): `window.EQ_ROSTER_ADAPTER` loaded while `window.getSiteName` was not, during the eager pre-login Dashboard render — the exact fallback-branch condition this fix targets, confirmed live rather than only inferred from source.
- Needed three rebases to land: `origin/main` moved 606→607(#834)→608(#838)→609(#835)→610(#839) while this PR was open, with two sibling PRs independently claiming this branch's exact next version number in a row. Resolved each time by re-fetching, rebasing, renumbering past main's current tip, and re-running the full verification suite — final number v3.5.611.
- eq-field [PR #836](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/836), squash-merged on Royce's explicit "merge everything", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- **Open follow-up**: not click-tested live against real MOD10/SLDC data — no live SKS/Core credentials in this environment, same standing limitation as PR #833. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #838 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.608 — FIX: Map / Roster Overview map tiles — CARTO now requires an API key we don't have)
- Both map views (`site-map.js`'s Map page, `roster-overview-map.js`'s optional Roster Overview map) rendered every tile as a grey "API KEY REQUIRED — carto.com/basemaps/apikey" watermark, site markers still plotting correctly on top. Verified live before touching code: fetched a real tile from the hardcoded `basemaps.cartocdn.com` URL both files used — 200 OK, but the image itself is the watermark. CARTO's free anonymous basemap tier now gates on a key neither file ever had; a provider-side change, not a regression in this codebase.
- FIX — swapped both call sites to OpenStreetMap's standard tile server (`tile.openstreetmap.org`), verified live (fetched a real tile, confirmed it renders actual map data, no key needed). Added the OSM attribution string neither file carried before — a pre-existing gap, since the CARTO tiles were shown completely unattributed.
- eq-field [PR #838](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/838), squash-merged on explicit "safely merge" (re-verified freshness/mergeability immediately before merging), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- **Standing risk, not a fix needed today**: OSM's own tile usage policy discourages heavy production use of its free anonymous server without a dedicated provider — this is a like-for-like free-tier swap, not a structural guarantee against the same class of failure recurring if map-tab usage grows. No monitoring exists for a third-party tile provider silently degrading again.
- Also confirmed this session: both follow-up tasks from the earlier MOD10 roster-alias fix (v3.5.606) are now merged — [PR #835](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/835) (v3.5.607, site-lead/contacts on an aliased My Schedule day) and [PR #836](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/836) (v3.5.611, Dashboard/Trial Dashboard's own site-lookup copy). Every known call site for that gap is now closed.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #831 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.605 — FIX: CSV people import silently nulled existing fields on every re-import)
- `importPeopleToSB()` (`scripts/supabase-entities.js`) matched a CSV row to an existing person by phone/email, then sent a full-row PATCH unconditionally — unlike `savePersonToSB` (the single Add/Edit Person path), which diffs against the pre-edit snapshot via `_diffRow` before patching. Any blank cell on a matched row nulled that field, across 7 optional columns (`phone, email, licence, agency, tafe_day, dob_day, dob_month, start_date`) — not just a missing column. The default CSV export always includes every column, so a strict export → edit → re-import round trip never showed this; any hand-built or partial CSV (HR export, email list) did.
- Checked ehow's `app_data.audit_log` + `audit_log_archive` for every `staff.start_date` non-null→null flip, ever, before assuming damage: found exactly one (Aiden Crowley, 2026-08-24), but it fingerprints as the OLD pre-`84f7bc72` `savePersonToSB` bug (`job_title` + all 3 `emergency_contact_*` fields flipped in the same write — fields this import path never touches), already closed by that earlier fix. No confirmed instance of this specific bug causing real data loss.
- Fix mirrors `_diffRow`'s guard on `savePersonToSB`: a matched row now only sends fields the CSV actually supplied a non-blank value for; unmatched (new) rows are unaffected. 3 new regression tests in `tests/import-people-merge.test.js` (9/9 passing).
- eq-field [PR #831](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/831), squash-merged on explicit "merge when safe" (mergeability + CI re-checked clean immediately before merge: `mergeable: CLEAN`, all checks green, branch already had latest main, diff scope matched exactly). Rebased onto origin/main after #832 (v3.5.604) merged first and independently claimed that version number — renumbered to v3.5.605. Confirmed live via Netlify's own deploy record (`commit_ref` matches merge commit `d91eebb3`, `state: ready`, `context: production`), not just the merge succeeding.
- **Open follow-up, not resolved this session**: this makes CSV import unable to *deliberately* blank a field on an existing person (e.g. bulk-clearing `agency` for someone who left a labour-hire firm) — a blank cell now always means "no info supplied," never "clear this." Flagged in the PR, not blocking. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #832 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.604 — SECURITY: apprentice self-assessment — a manager could rate an apprentice's own "self" score for them)
- Found while critiquing the Apprentices feature at Royce's request, following the prior day's journal-privacy fix (v3.5.600): `submitRating()`'s self-assessment branch let a manager submit a "self" rating on behalf of any apprentice, written identically to the apprentice's own answer with no record of who actually entered it — quietly undermining the Skills Passport's honest self-vs-supervisor comparison.

- Client-side: `apprentices-skills-passport.js`'s "How am I going?" button now only renders for the profile's own owner (`_isSelfProfile`) — previously shown to every viewer regardless of role, which would 403 on submit for a manager once the server fix landed.
- Added a regression case to `tests/apprentice-write-scoping.test.js` (manager `submit-self-assessment` on someone else's profile → 403) — the existing suite never asserted the manager-caller case for this action, which is how it shipped unnoticed.

## 2026-08-30 (PR #830 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.603 — timesheets gets roster's staleness guard preemptively; roster cells hint at the project-code shortcut)
- Timesheets' canonical save path (`_sbTimesheetsCanon`) has the identical blind whole-week delete+insert shape that caused v3.5.601's real live incident on roster's twin path — same tenant (`sks`), same architecture. Verified live before porting: `app_data.timesheets` has `updated_at`, row-level-only RLS, `sks` genuinely in `TIMESHEETS_CANONICAL_TENANTS`. Ported preemptively rather than waiting for an observed incident.
- `timesheets-adapter.js` gains `weekRevFingerprint()`/`isFingerprintAware()`; a timesheet day can hold multiple job segments (unlike roster's single value), so its fingerprint is the MAX `updated_at` across that day's segments — proven adversarially: a drift on just one of two same-day segments still correctly refuses the overwrite. `supabase-canon-write.js` gains `_weekStillFreshTs()`, a parallel (not shared) implementation to roster's own.
- Roster: a cell whose site has its own project code(s) now shows a plain hover tooltip ("Also accepts: MOD10") — the three live attempts to type a project code into the site cell earlier this session weren't user error, supervisors just had no way to know the shortcut existed. Zero layout footprint, independent of the `roster_project_picker` toggle.
- New `tests/timesheets-canon-write.test.js` (10 assertions) + 8 new assertions in `tests/timesheets-adapter.test.js` (now 96/96).

## 2026-08-30 (PR #829 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.601 + v3.5.602 — SKS canonical roster staleness guard + typed-project-code alias resolution)
- Root cause of a real live incident: `_sbRosterCanon`'s `_replaceWeek` always blind-deletes-and-reinserts a staff member's whole week from whatever's in the browser's memory, with no check against the live server state — a tab open across a server-side change silently clobbered it back the instant it next saved anything in that week. Happened for real, three times, to the same test rows (2026-08-29/30) while a data fix was being applied by hand.
- Fix (v3.5.601): `roster-adapter.js` gains a per-day `_rev` field + `weekRevFingerprint()`/`isFingerprintAware()`; `_replaceWeek` refuses the blind overwrite when the live fingerprint has moved since load, refetches real state, warns via toast instead. `realtime.js` gains the matching `_project`/`_rev` carry-through on a live patch (closes a separate gap where project-code changes never live-synced since v3.5.598). New `tests/supabase-canon-write.test.js` — first coverage this save path has ever had. Reverted the v3.5.599 hotfix's `site_project_id` key-omission now that its blocking migration is confirmed live.
- Feature (v3.5.602), prompted live by the same incident: typing a project code (e.g. "MOD10") directly into the roster's site cell now resolves both the real site and the project tag together — same never-guess collision rule the site-code resolver already uses. Royce: "Users will want to type MOD10 - how do we do that and make it reference SLDC site info for their schedule?"
- Ran `/decide` against three staged fix options before building; shipped the compare-and-swap guard plus the one slice of realtime it depends on, not the full tab-focus-refresh tier (flagged as still open).

## 2026-08-29 (PR #828 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.600 — SECURITY: apprentice journal — private entries leaked to every supervisor/manager)
- Royce asked to investigate apprentices-tab security ("only my account and the apprentice should see it"). Read the full in-HTML changelog history first — 9 prior security fixes to this exact feature already shipped between v3.5.502 and v3.5.527 — then verified today's actual state against live code and live ehow rather than trusting that history as still-current.
- Found `netlify/functions/apprentice-data.js`'s journal query used the same `byProfile()` filter as every other apprentice table, which resolves to no filter at all for manager/supervisor sessions — so it returned every apprentice's COMPLETE journal, private entries included, to any manager/supervisor account. `journal.js`'s own render function (line 106) already filters to `shared`-only for display, and its own header states the intended design plainly ("Private by default... managers only see shared entries") — the server-side fetch never enforced that, so the UI's filter was cosmetic against data that had already left the server.
- Fix: the journal fetch now applies `shared=eq.true` whenever the caller is manager/supervisor `eq_role`. Self view is unchanged. No DB/migration change needed — live-queried `information_schema.role_table_grants` on ehow first and confirmed all 7 personal apprentice tables already have zero `authenticated`/`anon` grants, so the two Netlify functions really are the only path in; the bug was purely in this one file's application logic.
- Added 2 targeted regression assertions to `tests/apprentice-data-scoping.test.js` (manager query IS filtered to `shared=eq.true`; self query is NOT) — the existing suite, written for the original 2026-08-18 fix, never actually checked the journal query's filter, which is how this shipped unnoticed for ~11 days.
- Not touched, by design: the other 6 apprentice tables' manager-sees-all model (confirmed intentional, pre-existing product behaviour, and matches Royce's own original 2026-08-18 ask — see `eq/pending/eq-field.md`) and the `eq` sandbox tenant's separate unscoped fallback path (documented, accepted, disposable demo content).
- eq-field [PR #828](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/828), squash-merged on explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.600`).
- Version renumbered twice mid-flight: v3.5.598 → v3.5.599 (sibling PR #826 claimed .598 first, caught pre-push) → v3.5.600 (PR #827's hotfix for #826 claimed .599 in between, caught on the pre-*merge* freshness check — not just pre-push).
- **Open follow-up, not resolved this session**: `apprentice-data.js` has no `audit_log` write, so there's no way to confirm whether any manager/supervisor account fetched another apprentice's private journal during the ~11-day exposure window. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-28 (PR #824 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.594 — Documents to Sign: raw-file fallback for a load failure that doesn't resolve on retry)
- Follow-on to the same-day PR #823 (v3.5.593, below): that fix stopped the spinner hanging forever on a failed `PDFViewerApplication.open()`, but "go back and try again" just re-ran the identical failing inline-viewer path — no real recovery if the failure was persistent (an expired signed URL, a genuinely bad conversion output) rather than transient. Spawned as `task_445c878c` by that session; this session is that task, started by Royce.
- `openSignDocumentViewer()`'s `opts` now carries `rawStoragePath` (the pre-conversion original file's Storage path), threaded through from `sign-documents.js`'s existing call site — the same value its old pre-v3.5.586 `window.open()`-a-raw-file button already used.
- `_signdocShowLoadError()` now renders an "Open original file ↗" button instead of a dead-end message whenever a `rawStoragePath` is on record. New `_signdocOpenRawFallback()` resolves a signed URL via `sbCreateSignedUrl('attachments', path, 300)`, then opens it from a second, fully synchronous click handler — required because iOS Safari silently blocks `window.open()` if any `await` sits between the click and the call (established pattern in this file family, v3.5.535/536). Opening the raw file still calls `_signMarkViewed()` and unlocks the Sign button, matching pre-v3.5.585 gate behaviour exactly.
- No fallback button (old plain message unchanged) when a document has no raw twin on record.
- Verified via an isolated harness (Node `vm`, loads the real edited file, drives the real trigger path — `PDFViewerApplication.open()` rejecting, not calling the private error handler directly): 14/14 assertions across the success, no-rawStoragePath, and signed-URL-failure cases.
- `check-cache-busters.mjs` caught real drift before commit: `app-state.js` is the one eagerly-loaded file among the changed set (the two sign-document files are lazy-loaded, auto-versioned) — its own static `<script>` tag in `index.html` needed a manual bump.
- eq-field [PR #824](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/824), squash-merged on explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` showing `v3.5.594`.

## 2026-08-28 (PR #821 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.596 — site contact info silently dead since v3.5.551)
- `_siteInternalContactsHtml()` (`scripts/roster.js`/`scripts/sites.js`) reads `primary_contact_name`/`primary_contact_phone`/`secondary_contact_name`/`secondary_contact_phone` off `STATE.sites`, called from My Schedule and the Sites screen. Shipped in PR #770 (v3.5.551) but never actually rendered anything, for any site, the entire time.
- Root cause: `index.html`'s boot-time fetch maps `app_data.field_sites` rows into `STATE.sites` via an explicit allowlist that never included these 4 fields — so the render function always saw `undefined` and returned `''`. Confirmed via `git grep` against the full current `origin/main`: nothing anywhere else ever wrote those keys onto a `STATE.sites` entry, so this is a mapping gap that's existed for the entire life of the feature, not a recent regression.
- Confirmed against live ehow data before writing the fix: 1 of 57 `app_data.field_sites` rows (Equinix SY5 — Matthew Miller / Scott Hotson, both with real phone numbers) has contact data set and would have been invisible the whole time.
- Fixed: added the 4 fields to the mapping alongside the existing `customer_name` pattern. Did not touch `scripts/roster.js`/`scripts/sites.js` (render logic already correct, already escapes via `esc()`) or the `SEED.sites` total-load-failure fallback mapping (separate, narrower, out of scope).
- Hit a live version collision at merge time: opened as v3.5.592, but `main` had advanced to v3.5.595 (3 unrelated Documents-to-Sign PRs) by the time of the actual merge — rebased, resolved conflicts across `index.html` (changelog banner + cache-buster tag), `scripts/app-state.js`, `sw.js`, and `docs/reflection-log.md`, renumbered to v3.5.596, and re-ran the full local check suite (33/33 tests, eslint 0 errors/917 pre-existing warnings unchanged, both drift guards clean) before force-pushing.
- eq-field [PR #821](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/821) (v3.5.596), squash-merged on explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- Not click-tested live — both tenants are Core-only for auth (the deploy preview itself shows the standalone PIN gate, a documented dead end for both), so real tenant data is only reachable through a live Core session, unavailable in this environment. Verified instead via a Node simulation of the exact fixed mapping against the real Equinix SY5 row shape, plus a clean deploy-preview boot (zero console errors, tenant-config resolved successfully).

Fill Week (v3.5.583) had already solved this, but only as its own separate row button — the natural click into a cell didn't share the fix.

Also absorbed an isolated, unrelated Netlify/GitHub SSH checkout failure on the first deploy-preview attempt (every other deploy that hour succeeded — infra flake, not the diff), resolved with a retry commit.
