## 2026-08-28 (PR #823 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.593 — Documents to Sign: fix silent-forever spinner on a failed PDF load)
- Royce relayed Luke Wheeler's report: he could sign one pushed document but the Schneider Contractor Safety Handbook "wouldn't load" — "pops open another window rather than preparing it for download." Investigated live-DB-first: `document_register` on ehow showed this document's own state correct (`pdf_status='ready'`, `pdf_storage_path` populated, the converted PDF genuinely present in Storage at the right size, 4.39MB) and 4 of Luke's 5 co-signers had already signed it successfully — ruled out a data problem before reading any client code.
- Root cause found in the code path instead: `scripts/sign-documents-viewer.js`'s `_signdocPollGate()` calls `PDFViewerApplication.open({url: opts.pdfUrl})` with no `.catch()`. Proved live — against production's deployed pdf.js, then again against the edited copy via a throwaway local test harness — that `open()` really can reject. Any genuine fetch failure (an expired signed URL, a network blip, a bad file from eq-shell's new PDF-conversion pipeline) left this as an unhandled promise rejection: the "Loading document…" spinner ran forever, nothing ever told the signer it had failed, and there was no way back to try anything else.
- Fixed: the rejection is now caught, polling stops, and a plain "Couldn't load this document — go back and try again" message replaces the spinner.
- **Root cause of Luke's specific report is not fully confirmed live** — no signer credentials in this environment to reproduce the exact fetch he hit. This closes a real, verified gap in the same code path; a retry on a fresh tab is needed to confirm it actually resolves his report.
- Follow-up flagged, not built this session (Royce approved the narrower spinner fix specifically): the new error state still has no functional fallback if a load failure turns out to be persistent rather than transient — no way back to the old raw-file/`window.open()` path once a document has a PDF twin. Spawned as a background task (`task_445c878c`), already started by Royce in a separate session.
- Incidental finding, not acted on: two other open PRs ([#821](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/821), [#822](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/822)) both claimed version "v3.5.592" simultaneously; this session used v3.5.593 to avoid the collision.

## 2026-08-28 (PR #818 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.589 / PR #819 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.590 — digest "Send test to myself": two more real bugs found and fixed)
- Royce's first real click-through on "Send test to myself" (Manage → Email Templates) failed with "No email on file for your account" despite his email showing correctly in the recipients checklist directly above the button. Root cause: `netlify/functions/send-digest-test.js` resolved the caller's identity via the `app_data.field_managers` VIEW, whose SQL bakes `tenant_id = (auth.jwt() -> 'app_metadata' ->> 'tenant_id')::uuid` directly into its WHERE clause — not reachable via any service-role/static-bearer credential, since `auth.jwt()` has no `app_metadata` claim in that context. Silently returned 0 rows for every caller, not just Royce. Fixed by querying `app_data.staff` directly instead, reproducing the view's own name/active-flag logic in JS. eq-field [PR #818](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/818) (v3.5.589), merged, live.
- Real email arrived next, but "the info is completely wrong." Root cause: `supabase/functions/supervisor-digest/index.ts` (a Deno edge function, a separate runtime from both the browser and Netlify Functions) queries `app_data.leave_requests.status` directly with Title-case literals (`"Approved"`, `"Pending"`) — but that column is a lowercase-only CHECK enum (`pending`/`approved`/`rejected`/`cancelled`, confirmed via `pg_get_constraintdef`). The two existing adapters that handle this exact split everywhere else in the app (`scripts/leave-adapter.js` browser-side, `netlify/functions/_shared/leave-canonical.js` server-side) were both already correct — this edge function is the one caller that talks to the table directly, with neither adapter in the loop. Both queries matched zero rows since the function's "v3.5.216 (canonical rewrite)" — every real Friday send has shown "Nobody approved off" and an empty pending-approvals section regardless of actual data, the entire time.
- Fixed both queries to filter on the correct lowercase enum values. eq-field [PR #819](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/819) (v3.5.590), merged, live — **and** the `supervisor-digest` edge function itself redeployed to production ehow (version 14→15, `verify_jwt: false` preserved, content-verified live post-deploy) since Supabase edge functions ship independently of Netlify/git and merging the PR alone would not have changed the live cron behaviour.
- Client-side app (Leave screen, roster overlay) was never affected — `leave.js`'s status comparisons run on data already normalized by `leave-adapter.js` before the client ever sees it. Only this one edge function, which bypasses both adapters, had the bug.
- Answers Royce's design question ("is the idea just to prove the digest works or should it be populated?") implicitly: the test-send has always been designed to use real computed data for the requesting supervisor (v3.5.573), not synthetic samples — the fix restores that intent rather than changing it.

## 2026-08-27 (PR #813 MERGED + LIVE — restore supervisor directory visibility for plain workers)
- Brendan Drinkwater (SKS worker) reported the Leave request form showing no supervisors in the approver dropdown. Reproduced live via a JWT-simulated read of his exact session claims (tenant/role/actor_id): `app_data.field_managers` returned 0 rows despite 20 real active supervisors existing on ehow — not personal to him, every non-manager/non-supervisor caller on both ehow and zaap saw the same empty list.
- Root cause: eq-shell's SEC-33 fix (migration 0256, PR #1510, applied 2026-08-21 to both planes) added a RESTRICTIVE own-row-or-manager SELECT policy on `app_data.staff` — correct and necessary, it protects DOB/address/emergency-contact/pay-rate PII. `app_data.field_managers`, a `security_invoker=on` view over that same table, silently inherited the restriction too, even though it exposes no sensitive columns (name/category/phone/email/role only). Live since 2026-08-21, ~6 days, both tenants.
- Fixed: `field_managers` becomes a deliberate, documented exception to this repo's "always `security_invoker=on`" rule (see CLAUDE.md's Schema gotchas) — flipped to `security_invoker=false` with an inline `tenant_id` filter added to the view's own WHERE clause, replacing the tenant isolation invoker+RLS used to provide for free. Safe only because this view has nothing sensitive to leak — contrast `app_data.field_people`, which does and correctly stays invoker=on (see that view's own separate fix below, PRs #814/#815/#817).
- Applied live to both `ehowgjardagevnrluult` and `zaapmfdkgedqupfjtchl` via Supabase MCP ahead of the PR, on Royce's explicit "apply to both." No client-side code changes — Field already reads this exact view.
- Same fix incidentally restored Recognitions, Incidents notify, Sites lead display, Teams display, and CC-on-leave-emails for plain workers — all read the same `STATE.managers`/`field_managers` data source.
- Verified via a fresh JWT simulation after applying (separate from the apply itself): Brendan's real claims now return 20 rows (was 0); a bogus tenant_id returns 0 on both planes (cross-tenant isolation intact); zaap's own positive case reads 0 too, confirmed pre-existing and correct (zaap has 0 `is_supervisor` rows tenant-wide — disposable sandbox, no data — not a fix failure).
- Not yet click-tested live by Brendan himself — planned in person, 2026-08-28.
- Follow-on found by a later, separate session: the inline `tenant_id = auth.jwt()...` filter this fix introduced is unreachable from a `service_role` caller (no JWT claims in that context), which broke the digest "Send test to myself" feature until PR #818 (below) routed that caller around the view instead.

## 2026-08-27 (PR #816 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.588 — Documents to Sign: inline PDF viewer, root cause found and fixed)
- PR #811 (v3.5.586) shipped the inline PDF.js viewer, replacing `window.open()`-the-raw-file. It rendered a toolbar but never loaded any document, any size — PDF.js's own `validateFileURL` guard rejects a `?file=` URL whose origin doesn't match the viewer's, which a signed Supabase Storage URL always fails. A loading spinner shipped first as a hedge while the cause was still open; Royce's DevTools Console screenshot then surfaced the real error text.
- Fixed by dropping `?file=` and driving the load via `PDFViewerApplication.open({url})` — PDF.js's own API for a host page supplying the URL, which doesn't call `validateFileURL` at all. Verified live by exercising the vendored viewer's JS directly.
- Companion eq-shell fixes same day: Office→PDF conversion pipeline (self-hosted Gotenberg, PR #1635) so there's a same-origin-convertible PDF to show in the first place, and a Register-tab staleness fix (PR #1644) after Royce reported a pushed document "disappearing" (it hadn't — the tab just never refetched).
- Follow-on: mapped out what "push this to everyone" actually requires (5 role-pushes, no bulk "all" option) and pulled a live, named list of the 14 SKS staff still unreachable by any push (no linked Shell login). Both recorded in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`, not fixed this session — Royce's own outreach/workflow, not a code gap.

## 2026-08-26 (PR #809 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.584 — SKS canonical roster: fix inert realtime sync + register local writes)
- Found while investigating the Ctrl+Enter fix below: SKS's canonical roster (`app_data.schedule_entries`, one row per person per day) streams realtime changes as NARROW per-day rows, but `realtime.js`'s `_rtApplyChange()` only knew how to parse the legacy WIDE `schedule` shape (one row per person per week) — a second supervisor's live roster edit never reached the first supervisor's screen for SKS specifically, silently falling back to the 30s poll instead.
- Fixed: new `_rtApplyCanonicalScheduleChange()` translates a narrow `schedule_entries` INSERT/UPDATE into the existing wide `STATE.schedule` cell shape via `roster-adapter.js`'s own helpers (`dateToWeekDay`, `staffIdToName`, `cellFromRow`, `RS_DAYS`), dispatched from `_rtApplyChange()` when `EQ_ROSTER_ADAPTER.isCanonicalRosterTenant(true)`. DELETE events are a deliberate no-op — `_replaceWeek`'s delete-then-insert pattern always follows a DELETE with a re-INSERT, so acting on the DELETE alone would just blank the cell for a moment.
- Companion fix, same investigation: `supabase-canon-write.js`'s `_replaceWeek` switched its POST from `return=minimal` to `return=representation` and now calls `_rtMarkLocalWrite(row.schedule_id)` for every inserted row (both the normal insert path and the audit-Revert PATCH/POST-fallback branch) — without this, a save's own realtime echo had no id to suppress and the fix above would have double-applied a writer's own change back onto itself.
- Version collision: this PR's own commit/PR title still literally reads "v3.5.583" — it collided with PR #808 (also 583) mid-flight, was renumbered to 584 and re-verified, but the title text was never re-edited after the rebase. Confirmed the live/shipped version is 584 by reading `APP_VERSION` straight off `origin/main`, not the PR title — the two disagree here, trust the file.
- **Does not close the other half of the same investigation.** `roster-adapter.js`'s `toWideList()` (the read/hydrate path) still never sets `id`/`updated_at` on the wide rows it builds — re-confirmed live against `origin/main` before writing this entry, not assumed. SKS roster saves still can't tell if someone else changed the row first; the "someone else is editing this" conflict warning still can't fire for SKS. Left open: `eq/pending/eq-field.md`, `task_867a4c80`.
- Not click-tested live (no authenticated SKS session in this environment, same standing limitation as most entries in this file) — verified via direct trace against the live shipped files instead.

## 2026-08-27 (PR #807 MERGED + LIVE — schedule_entries roster writes now stamp created_by/updated_by)
- `app_data.schedule_entries` (Design B canonical roster, ehow/SKS) had no actor attribution on write — every row from a real Batch Fill roster edit carried `created_by`/`updated_by` both `null`, despite `audit_log` correctly naming the acting manager for the same action. No FK-backed way to tell who changed a roster row without guessing by timestamp proximity against `audit_log`.
- Fixed at the DB layer: a new `BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE` trigger (`schedule_entries_stamp_actor`) stamps both columns via the already-live `app_data.eq__caller_actor_staff_id(tenant_id)` helper — the same identity `timesheets`/`leave_requests` RLS already resolves, same underlying actor `audit_log`'s `x-eq-actor` header carries, just read via the JWT claim instead. No JS change — `roster-adapter.js` untouched, the trigger covers every write path to the table, not just Batch Fill.
- Live-verified, not just reasoned through: confirmed the column shape (`uuid`, no FK — `supervisor_id`'s existing FK to `app_data.staff.staff_id` on the same table confirmed the assumed target) before applying. After applying, one real write test with a resolvable actor claim (`set_config('request.jwt.claims', ...)`, the same GUC PostgREST sets per request) stamped both columns to that actor's own `staff_id` exactly; a second test with no claim left both `NULL`, no error — matches today's fail-closed behaviour. Test rows deleted immediately after capturing results, confirmed zero left. `get_advisors(security)` re-run post-apply — no new finding.
- Deploy verified live via exact Netlify `commit_ref` match on the production deploy record. No version bump — PR touched only `supabase/migrations/*.sql` and `docs/reflection-log.md`, no client-side files.
- Not yet click-tested through the real roster UI — same standing sandbox limitation as most entries in this file. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-27 (PR #808 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.583 — Timesheets: Fill Week button, dedicated Approved column, narrower Name column)
- Royce: Phoenix Khatri showed "OFF" on Timesheets with no leave request to be found — traced live to Luke Wheeler batch-filling his roster week directly (`audit_log`: "Filled Mon–Fri with OFF", 2026-08-20), not a Leave Requests bug. Timesheets reads the raw roster/schedule cell directly and never consults `leave_requests` — roster OFF and an approved leave request are two disconnected mechanisms that render identically. Nothing to fix there; Royce's follow-up confirmed the real cause was a workplace injury, logged as roster code INJURED instead, no leave request either way.
- ADD — "Fill week" row button (desktop Timesheets grid, `scripts/timesheets-spans.js`): a normal click only covers one contiguous run of same-state days, so a locked leave/TAFE day mid-week forced two separate edits. Opens one editor spanning every currently-workable weekday, skipping locked days automatically, existing job/hours merged in per day. Weekend is opt-in via an in-modal checkbox, not included by default. Hidden on rows with nothing workable to fill.
- ADD — dedicated "Approved" column on the same grid, replacing a faint 11px chip crammed next to the Grp badge (`scripts/timesheets.js`: new `_tsApprovalButton`, additive — `_tsApprovalChip` untouched, still used by the mobile renderer + the legacy desktop fallback table). Same `toggleTsApproval()` click.
- Name column narrowed 110px → 88px (20%) on the same grid to give the new column room.
- Hit a real merge conflict on landing (main had moved — PR #805 `v3.5.582` landed first). Rebased clean; post-rebase re-verification caught 3 stale `v3.5.582` comment references (fixed) and a real `max-lines` breach on `timesheets.js` (2,410 vs. a 2,400 grandfathered ceiling) — closed via comment compression + a documented 50-increment bump to 2,450 (`eslint.config.js`), same escape-valve convention as `leave.js`/`apprentices.js`/`tender-pipeline.js`.
- Verified via an isolated harness loading the real edited files/dependencies, plus full local suite (lint 0 errors, build-bundles, cache-busters, 33/33 tests). GitHub's `mergeable` field stalled at `UNKNOWN` for over a minute post-push (confirmed via raw API, not a `gh` cache artifact) — merged directly rather than continuing to poll it; went through clean. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- Full detail (including the two still-open UX rough edges and the standing "OFF ≠ approved leave" display gap, not fixed) in `eq/pending/eq-field.md` and `sessions/2026-08-27.md`.

## 2026-08-26 (PR #805 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.582 — Edit Roster: fix Ctrl+Enter not filling on the first press)
- Royce, from a screenshot: Fill Week "looks like it saves but then [Tue-Fri go blank]", and Ctrl+Enter "sends me to the next row, then if i click on the original row and press control enter it fills the row" — the first Ctrl+Enter press on a fresh row silently did nothing; a second press (after re-focusing the same row) worked.
- Root cause: a race, not a logic bug. `updateCell()` (`roster.js`) fires `_rosterGateAssignment()` (`people-required-credentials.js`, unconditionally async — it re-checks a feature-toggle cache via `.then()` on every call, even when already warm) and returned immediately without awaiting it. `roster-editor-keyboard.js`'s Ctrl+Enter handler called `fillWeek()`/`_focusPersonDay()` synchronously right after `updateCell()`, so on a first press it ran before the gate check — and the actual cell commit — had resolved, reading a Monday value that hadn't been written yet.
- Fixed by making the promise chain real: `updateCell()` now `return`s the `_rosterGateAssignment().then(...)` chain instead of letting it run detached; the keyboard handler sequences `fillWeek()`/`_focusPersonDay()` inside `Promise.resolve(commit).then(...)` instead of calling them immediately.
- Investigated the separately-reported "Fill Week looks like it saves but then reverts" (the mouse `⇒wk` button) — no data-loss bug found; the exact people/week from the screenshot checked directly against live ehow, every row had correct Mon-Fri data. Most likely the screenshot caught the grid mid-traversal during a real multi-minute Ctrl+Enter burst, not an actual revert.
- Hit the `max-lines` ratchet on `roster.js` (1753/1750) from the fix's own explanatory comment — trimmed to 2 lines to land back under the ceiling (1749 final).
- Merged on Royce's "merge when safe" after a version collision with PR #806 (Preferred Name, unrelated, also claimed v3.5.581) — rebased, renumbered to v3.5.582, full check suite re-run before force-push. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- Same investigation surfaced two SKS canonical-roster gaps not fixed by this PR — see the 2026-08-26 PR #809 entry above (one closed, one still open) and `sessions/2026-08-26.md`.
- Not click-tested live (no authenticated SKS session in this environment) — verified via isolated harness against the real edited files.

Royce: "go ahead and build it."

## 2026-08-26 (PR #792 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.572 — fillWeek() protects pre-entered days from Ctrl+Enter overwrite)
- Royce asked whether "Copy Last Week" overwrites roster days workers have already filled in ahead of time. It doesn't — `copyLastWeek()` (batch.js) has only ever filled empty cells. But the question surfaced that `fillWeek()` (what Ctrl+Enter calls) had the opposite, unprotected behaviour: it unconditionally overwrote Tue–Fri with Monday's value, skipping only TAFE/education days. A day someone had already entered was silently clobbered — arguably the more urgent version of the exact risk Royce was asking about, since Ctrl+Enter is used far more often than Copy Last Week.
- Surfaced explicitly and confirmed via AskUserQuestion before changing live, weekly-used behaviour rather than patching it silently. Royce: "Protect existing entries" (match `copyLastWeek()`'s fill-empty-only rule) + "Hold for now" (no dedicated Fill-week button as Ctrl+Enter backup insurance — Tab already fills a row without a mouse).
- `fillWeek()` now skips any day that already holds a value, in addition to the existing TAFE-day skip. Toast reflects what was skipped and why. Verified via an isolated Node test running the actual shipped function body — 17/17 assertions across 6 scenarios, including exact-wording regression checks on the pre-existing TAFE-skip and common-case toast text.
- Full test suite, lint (0 errors, 908 warnings unchanged from baseline), bundle check, and cache-buster check all clean. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #790 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.571 — Edit Roster: name-column alignment fix + Ctrl+Enter vs the site-code dropdown)
- Closes out the Ctrl+Enter regression left open at an earlier close: Royce confirmed via two screenshots that the site-code autocomplete dropdown does appear while typing in a roster cell — the leading hypothesis for why Ctrl+Enter's focus-jump silently no-op'd for him. Fixed by registering the keydown handler in the capture phase (`roster-editor-keyboard.js`), so it runs ahead of the native `<input list="site-datalist">` handling instead of getting swallowed by it.
- Same message also reported the name column's width shifting depending on whether a roster cell was filled. Live-measured before touching anything: `.editor-name` had `min-width:160px`/`max-width:200px` with no fixed width, so it sized to its own text content — a 2-character name produced a ~39px narrower column than a 27-character name, shifting every day-column's position and width in that row. Fixed with a fixed 200px width; re-measured identical across short and long names after.
- The capture-phase mechanism itself was verified via a real dispatched `KeyboardEvent` through the real listener (`preventDefault()` confirmed firing) — full end-to-end (commit + fill + focus jump together) was not re-verified this pass, judged low-risk since the underlying commit/fill/focus-jump logic was unchanged from its own prior same-day verification; only the listener's registration phase changed.
- Full test suite, lint (0 new warnings), bundle check, and cache-buster check all clean (caught and fixed real drift on `base.css` + `app-state.js`). Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #788 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.569 — observability.js: stop reporting local dev-server errors to production Sentry)
- Investigated Sentry issue `EQ-FIELD-Y` ("_ROSTER_WEEKDAYS already declared", flagged on the prior session-close card as "a rare crash, 2 people since July") before writing any code.
- Both lifetime occurrences trace to a local dev-server test session, not a real user — confirmed via each event's own tags: 2026-07-27 was `environment:development`, `url:http://localhost:8890/` (a prior session's own local test harness, already flagged as noise in that session's own reflection log at the time); 2026-08-25 was this session's own earlier manual test-script injection against real production, confirmed via matching `cid`/`tenant_slug`/`embedded`/source-URL tags.
- `observability.js`'s own header comment already claimed "inert on previews/local" — the code never actually implemented that for `development`. `SENTRY_DSN` is a hardcoded public key with no environment gate, so any local dev server reports straight into the same shared production Sentry project as real users.
- Fix: `_report()`'s DSN checks now also bail when `_env() === 'development'`, matching the file's own already-stated intent. `preview`/`production` reporting is untouched.
- Live-verified against the actual local dev server this noise came from: `window.EQ_OBS.enabled === false` on localhost with the fix live, and `EQ_OBS.captureException()` produces zero network requests to Sentry's ingest endpoint (previously the same call from a local host successfully posted to production Sentry).
- Sentry issue `EQ-FIELD-Y` marked resolved with an explanatory comment.
- eq-field [PR #788](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/788), squash-merged, confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` and direct bundle-byte inspection.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #789 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.570 — FIX: TAFE Holidays config could silently lose ranges when added quickly)
- Royce reported adding 4 TAFE holiday date ranges and having them not save properly.
- Root cause: rapid Add clicks fired overlapping, unserialized saves — each captured the in-memory list at its own start time, and whichever save finished last (not necessarily last-clicked) won the database row. The screen always showed all 4 ranges (rendered from memory), so nothing looked wrong until a reload exposed the gap.
- Fixed by chaining every save through one promise so each queued save reads the list only once its turn arrives, after every earlier add has landed. A secondary bug (an always-redundant duplicate-create request after every save, from a misread database response) was fixed in the same pass.
- Proved with an isolated regression test running the real save code 20 times under randomized network timing: the old logic lost data in roughly half the runs, the fixed logic lost none. Test committed as a permanent regression guard.
- eq-field [PR #789](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/789), squash-merged on explicit "yes", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #786 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.568 — FIX: preferred-name mapper drift + Full Name data-loss risk)
- Within minutes of the Preferred Name feature (below) going live, Royce hit Edit Person on Mohammed Hussain's own real record and found it broken: Full Name showed "Nabeel" instead of "Mohammed Hussain", Preferred Name and Job Title both showed blank despite both being set moments earlier.
- Root cause: `_mapPeopleRows()`'s field allowlist had drifted out of sync with `app_data.field_people` — missing `preferred_name` plus 5 pre-existing fields `editPerson()` has always tried to populate (`first_name`/`last_name`/`job_title`/`emergency_contact_mobile`/`emergency_contact_relationship`). Full Name was also being read from the preferred-name-coalesced `p.name` instead of raw first/last — an unchanged Save would have overwritten `first_name`/`last_name` with "Nabeel" for real.
- Confirmed live via direct query that the database itself was never wrong throughout — a pure client-side display bug, no data was actually lost.
- Verified end-to-end against the real shipped code, not just an isolated script: forced the real `people.js` to load past the login gate in a deploy preview, injected a row shaped like Mohammed Hussain's actual record, called the real `editPerson()`, and read every field back off the real DOM — Full Name, Preferred Name, Job Title, Mobile, Email, and all three Emergency Contact fields all came back correct.
- Landed at v3.5.568 after three same-day version-collision rebases — against #787, #785, and #780 in turn, each independently claiming this branch's version number before it could merge.
- eq-field [PR #786](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/786), squash-merged on explicit "yes", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #780 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.567 — Edit Roster week-picker: pin a "Current: ..." row when paged away)
- Royce, from a screenshot: the week-picker dropdown's visible window didn't include the currently-selected week after paging via Earlier/Later, no visible answer to "where am I." Hand-computed date arithmetic confirmed the popover's paging math was already correct and matched the screenshot exactly — the real gap was that the existing `.current` bold-highlight (v3.5.400) has nothing to attach to once the active week scrolls out of the rendered window.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #787 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.565 — Edit Roster: fix Ctrl+Enter losing focus on Monday)
- Live regression report right after Tier 2 shipped: "not even control enter works when entering on a monday" + "table isn't aligned. All over the shop."
- Root cause: `fillWeek()` (called by Ctrl+Enter) always calls `renderEditor()`, which replaces `#editor-content`'s entire `innerHTML` — detaching the input element the keydown handler held a reference to. `_updateCellCommit()` does the same conditionally, for multi-job sites. The old focus-jump helper's `indexOf()` against the rebuilt DOM could never match the stale reference, so it silently no-oped every time on Ctrl+Enter (100% reproducible) and intermittently on plain Enter.
- Data was never at risk — the Monday value and Tue-Fri fill both saved correctly throughout (state-layer, not DOM-dependent). Only the focus jump was lost.
- Fixed by resolving who's next by name before the commit/fill, then re-querying the fresh DOM by name+day after — a name survives a re-render, a DOM node reference doesn't.
- Checked the "table isn't aligned" half too: computed styles for the grid's columns read identical across every row shape — no CSS regression found.
- **Still reported broken by Royce after this deployed** ("cursor didn't move" on a clean re-test) — see `eq/pending/eq-field.md` for the open investigation; leading unconfirmed hypothesis is the site-code `<datalist>` autocomplete swallowing the Enter keypress.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #785 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.566 — People save: only patch fields that actually changed)
- Real incident: Royce corrected Zemi Asri's Group to Direct and start date via Edit Person; save succeeded, but employment_type reverted to Labour Hire ~6s later. Root cause: `savePersonToSB()` unconditionally resent all 19 columns on every save — eq-shell's Staff-page edit panel had the identical shape and clobbered this specific write with a stale snapshot on an unrelated field save.
- New `_diffRow()` helper (`scripts/supabase-entities.js`) diffs the outgoing PATCH against the record the modal was opened from and sends only what changed. Side effect: `archived`/`pin`/`dob` are now also protected against the same clobber.
- Fourth known incident of this exact bug class (Brian Griffin-Colls DOB overwrite 2026-08-17, Mohammed Hussain blocked-save 2026-08-18, the ehow `staff_derive_dob_from_cards` locked-edit guard PR #767/#769 2026-08-24) — the first three were each band-aided one field at a time; this fix closes the actual source.
- Paired fix on eq-shell (`SplitPanel.tsx`/`StaffPage.tsx`, `buildStaffPatch`) — eq-shell [PR #1590](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-shell/pull/1590), open, blocked on an unrelated pre-existing CI check (see eq-shell changelog).
- Branch landed at v3.5.566 after two rebases — 3 other PRs (#783/#784/#787) merged concurrently, one an urgent live hotfix. Caught mid-fix: `app-state.js`'s own `index.html` script tag needs its own version bump separate from `supabase-entities.js`'s — missed initially, `check-cache-busters.mjs` caught it before push.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #771 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.553 — Prestart/Toolbox/Diary: "+ Add by name" doing nothing in the Shell iframe)
- Royce reported real SKS crew feedback: clicking "+ Add by name" on Prestart did nothing — no dialog, no error. Root cause: `addPrestartCrewManual()` (and the identical `addDiaryAttendanceManual`/`addToolboxAttendeeManual`) called native `window.prompt()`. Confirmed against eq-shell's actual `FieldIframe.tsx` source, not assumed from general Chrome behaviour: the Field iframe is sandboxed without `allow-modals`, so per the HTML spec `prompt()`/`confirm()`/`alert()` inside it are required to return null/false immediately, no dialog, no exception — deterministic, not a browser-version heuristic.
- Fixed all three instances in one pass, not just the reported one — same bug, three places. New `createNamePromptController()` in `site-reports-shared.js`, matching the existing `createSignatureController` injection pattern; same validation as before (trim, 80-char cap, empty bail) at every call site.
- Verified via a real (uncommitted) Playwright check that stubs `window.prompt` to throw and confirms it's never called, run twice — once locally, once against the live deploy preview once CI went green.
- First instance of what turned out to be a whole bug class (BUG-009, already tracked in this repo) — see the entries below for PRs #773–#777, the wider sweep this one triggered.
- eq-field [PR #771](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/771), squash-merged on explicit "merge 771," confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.553`).

## 2026-08-25 (PR #773 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.555 — Prestart/Toolbox: Submit silently did nothing when a completeness gap was flagged, inside the Shell iframe)
- `confirmSubmitWithGaps()` (`site-reports-shared.js`) called `window.confirm()` to ask "N items may be incomplete. Submit anyway?" before letting a Prestart or Toolbox with a flagged gap through. eq-shell's `FieldIframe.tsx` sandboxes the Field iframe without `allow-modals`, and per the HTML spec `confirm()` there returns `false` immediately with no dialog. SKS is Core-only, so any supervisor submitting a flagged Prestart/Toolbox via the normal Shell-embedded path was silently blocked from submitting at all. Same BUG-009 family as v3.5.553's `createNamePromptController` fix (PR #771), whose own changelog flagged this exact function as "not touched this pass."
- Fix: `confirmSubmitWithGaps()` is now async and routes through the shared `#modal-confirm` dialog already used by `leave.js`/`timesheets.js`/`sks-pipeline*.js`/`batch.js` (mirrors `_leaveConfirm`'s pattern), falling back to `window.confirm()` if the modal markup is somehow absent. Both call sites (`submitPrestart`, `submitToolbox`) now `await` it — both were already `async`.
- Swept the 7 files PR #771's changelog had already flagged as "not yet converted" for the same bug class — all 7 still had at least one live-blocking call. Rather than bundling all of it into one diff, spun off 3 separate follow-up sessions: closed out below in PR #776 (`audits.js`), PR #774, and PR #775/#777.
- Version collision mid-flight: PR #772 (leave.js file-size split) landed `v3.5.554` first while this branch was in flight — rebased and renumbered `3.5.554 → 3.5.555`.
- Verified: `node --check`/eslint/full test suite clean; live-DOM behavioural test against the real production `#modal-confirm` markup + real `openModal`/`closeModal` (0/1/2-gap wording, Cancel/Submit-anyway, no handler leak); real Netlify deploy preview smoke-tested post-open. Not click-tested through the real Shell iframe with live SKS credentials — no live SKS session available in this environment.
- eq-field [PR #773](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/773), squash-merged on explicit "merge #773," confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.555`).

## 2026-08-25 (PRs #775/#777 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.558/v3.5.559 — BUG-009 sweep closed out: audit.js, apprentices.js, journal.js, timesheets-export.js, tender-pipeline.js, plus a real name-collision fix)
- Closed out the 5 remaining files PR #773's own body had spun off as follow-ups: `audit.js` (Audit Log Revert + its concurrent-edit re-confirm), `apprentices.js` (add/remove custom skill; also fixed in passing, `resolveFollowUp()`'s note was silently dropped when Shell-embedded — non-blocking before, but now a supervisor can actually attach one), `journal.js` (delete entry), `timesheets-export.js` (CSV import — reuses `timesheets.js`'s existing `_tsConfirm` rather than duplicating it), `tender-pipeline.js` (push a won tender's labour curve to the live roster — `_quickPushToSchedule()` is now `async`, it wasn't before). Shipped as eq-field [PR #775](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/775) (v3.5.558).
- Live-verified the actual shipped code against the real PR #775 deploy preview, not just source review: the `_auditConfirm`-pattern helper and the new `_apPromptText` prompt-modal (the one genuinely new piece of code in this pass — the other 4 files reuse the already-proven `_leaveConfirm` shape) were both invoked directly in a real browser against the real deployed bundle, working around this repo's own documented Core-only auth dead-end for standalone/preview surfaces (audit.js is core-loaded, reachable pre-auth; apprentices.js has no boot-time app-context dependency, so the real deployed file could be injected directly). Confirm/cancel/enter-key/empty-submit/repeat-use all checked against real DOM behaviour.

This app loads every script into one shared global scope, so two top-level functions sharing a name is a genuine bug (whichever file loads second silently overwrites the other's global), harmless today only because both implementations happen to be identical. Renamed to `_auditLogConfirm`.
- **That rename did not survive PR #775's own squash-merge** — a second concurrent session finishing the same branch from an earlier snapshot won the push race, and the merge landed with the collision still live even though the rest of the PR (5-file fix, version stamps, eslint ratchet bumps) came through correctly. Caught by diffing `origin/main`'s actual post-merge content directly, not by assuming a `MERGED` status meant the intended diff landed. Re-applied as its own PR, eq-field [PR #777](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/777) (v3.5.559).
- `origin/main` moved 4 times during the merge attempt alone (PRs #772/#773/#774/#776, each an unrelated concurrent BUG-009-family fix independently claiming the "next" version number) — 3 separate rebase-and-renumber rounds before the version number stopped colliding; confirmed via `gh pr list` the branch was the only one still open before finishing each round.
- Both PRs merged on Royce's explicit "continue/merge." This closes every file PR #773's original 7-file follow-up list named (`timesheets-approvals.js` was independently closed the same day by a concurrent PR #774, its own entry directly below) — nothing outstanding from that sweep. Full verification each round: `node --check`, full `tests/*.test.js` (30/30), `eslint@9` (0 errors), `check-cache-busters.mjs`, `build-bundles.mjs --check`, all clean. Production (`field.eq.solutions/sw.js`) confirmed at `v3.5.559` post-merge.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #776 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.556 — Site Audits: Submit silently did nothing when a non-conformance was flagged, inside the Shell iframe)
- `audits.js`'s `submitAudit()` called `window.confirm()` to gate submission when a non-conformance was flagged without corrective-action details. eq-shell's Field iframe sandboxes without `allow-modals`, so `confirm()` there returns `false` immediately with no dialog — any supervisor submitting a flagged audit via the normal Shell-embedded path (the only way real SKS users reach Field, since SKS is Core-only) was silently blocked from submitting at all.
- Same BUG-009 family as PR #773 (v3.5.555, same day), whose own changelog named this exact function as the deferred follow-up. Fix: new local `_auditConfirm()` helper mirroring `leave.js`'s `_leaveConfirm` / `teams.js`'s `_teamsConfirm`, routed through the existing shared `#modal-confirm` dialog. Kept local rather than calling into `SiteReportsShared` — `audits.js` is lazy-loaded alone via the `'audits'` tab-group bundle, where `site-reports-shared.js` isn't guaranteed loaded (only the `'safety'` bundle pulls both), so that dependency would have been a live crash risk for anyone reaching Submit via the plain Audits tab. `submitAudit()` was already `async`; the one call site now `await`s the helper.
- Version picked live at `3.5.556` after `origin/main` moved three times during the session's own investigation (an unrelated merge, then PR #773 itself merging mid-session) — re-verified immediately before each commit-adjacent step rather than trusting an earlier snapshot.
- Verified: `node --check` clean, full `tests/*.test.js` — 31/31 pass, `eslint@9` — 0 errors, `check-cache-busters.mjs` — clean. Live-DOM behavioural test against the real deploy preview (not just unit tests): modal opens with correct title/message/button text for both singular/plural grammar branches, Submit-anyway resolves `true`, Cancel resolves `false`, cleanup leaves no handler leak across repeat calls, zero console errors. Not click-tested through the real Shell iframe with live SKS credentials — same accepted limitation as PR #773.

## 2026-08-25 (PR #772 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.554 — leave.js file-size debt: email templates split into leave-email.js)
- `leave.js` was flush against its eslint `max-lines` grandfather ceiling (1899/1900, zero headroom) after #768 — the next PR touching the file would break CI with no room to absorb even a one-line fix. `triggerLeaveEmail()` (the 3 email templates) + `resendLeaveEmail()` (manual resend) — the single biggest, most self-contained block in the file — extracted into new `scripts/leave-email.js`, same file-size-convention split already used on timesheets.js/apprentices.js/people.js/roster.js/auth.js. Pure extraction, no behaviour change.
- Extracted byte-for-byte via a small Node script keyed on two unique marker comments (grepped first to confirm uniqueness, sanity-checked immediately before/after each marker before writing anything) rather than hand-retyped — the block contains emoji and em-dashes easy to corrupt by hand. `leave.js`: 1899 → 1724 lines; eslint ratchet lowered 1900 → 1750, rounded up to the nearest 50 per the block's own existing convention.
- `lazy-loader.js`'s `'leave'` tab entry gained `leave-email.js`; `core-bundle-b1.js` (mirrors `lazy-loader.js`) regenerated via `build-bundles.mjs --write`. `leave.js` itself is lazy-loaded and not tagged in `index.html`, so `check-cache-busters.mjs` doesn't apply to it directly — but `core-bundle-b1.js` IS statically tagged, so that tag needed a manual bump.
- Verified live on the deploy preview before merge, not just CI: `typeof triggerLeaveEmail === 'function'`, `typeof resendLeaveEmail === 'function'`, `scripts/leave-email.js?v=3.5.554` confirmed actually fetched by the browser via `lazy-loader.js`, `leaveCCList`/`leaveRequests` (leave.js's own module state) readable from the split file — zero `ReferenceError`, the exact failure class this file's own comments warn about repeatedly for cross-file lazy-loaded dependencies.
- **Version collision mid-session**: picked `3.5.553` (confirmed free via `git fetch` + `git tag -l`), built the full extraction against it, then found PR #771 had landed `3.5.553` first — same three version-stamp files, different reason. Reconciled via `git stash -u` → `git merge --ff-only origin/main` → `git stash pop` (one real conflict, index.html's changelog-banner insertion point) → renumbered every reference this session had written to `3.5.554` → re-ran the full verification pass (bundle drift, cache-busters, eslint, all 30 tests) a second time.
- Full test suite (30/30), eslint (0 errors), bundle-drift and cache-buster checks all clean both before and after the version renumber. `docs/reflection-log.md` entry added in the same commit.

## 2026-08-24 (PR #769 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.550 — Edit Person: reject a locked birthday edit instead of silently discarding it)
- Follow-up to #767 (same session, same day). That fix stopped a direct `dob_day`/`dob_month` edit from silently nulling out when a Cards-linked person had no `date_of_birth`. It deliberately left one case alone: a Cards-linked person who already has a real `date_of_birth` still had a direct day/month edit silently discarded — same trap, narrower group.
- `eq-shell`'s own Staff-edit path (`entity-patch.ts`) already hit this and already fixed it, with a named incident on record: "this exact silent-overwrite is what left Brian Griffin-Colls and 7 others with a 'birthday' but no real date of birth (2026-08-17)". Shell's fix rejects the edit up front with a clear message instead of letting it look like it worked. This PR ports the same guard into `staff_derive_dob_from_cards()`, gated on the value actually changing — the same gating Shell's own fix needed after a false-positive regression, since Field's `savePersonToSB()` also unconditionally resends `dob_day`/`dob_month` on every save regardless of what was actually edited.
- `people.js`'s save-failure toast now recognizes this specific rejection (`dob_locked_to_cards`) and explains it, instead of the generic "Save failed — check connection".
- Verified with 4 scenarios directly against ehow before any client code was written: reject-on-real-change (confirmed rolled back), allow-on-unrelated-field-resave, no regression on #767's null-DOB fix, exact error-message content. Full local suite green (`tests/*.test.js`, `eslint@9` 0 errors, cache-buster + bundle-drift both clean); deploy preview smoke-checked (boots clean at the new version, zero console errors) — full click-through of the actual reject path not done, no authenticated SKS session available this session.
- `people.js`'s grandfathered CI line cap bumped 1550→1600 for the added branch (7 lines replacing 1) — same escape valve already used for `apprentices.js`, not a new pattern.

## 2026-08-24 (PR #767 MERGED + LIVE — Birthday day/month root-caused + fixed: closes the `task_bb6cab43` thread)
- Continuation of tonight's earlier "birthday still broken" investigation (background task `task_bb6cab43`) — that pass's thorough client-side code read found nothing wrong and handed off with a clean "already ruled out" list. This session confirmed all of it was correct and found the actual cause one layer deeper.
- Root cause: `app_data.staff` (ehow) carries a second, undocumented `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger, `staff_derive_dob_from_cards` — never recorded in any eq-field migration or commit — that unconditionally re-derives `dob_day`/`dob_month` from the separate `date_of_birth` column whenever `cards_worker_id IS NOT NULL`. It fires as a side effect of `field_people_iud()`'s own inner `UPDATE app_data.staff`, immediately overwriting the value that statement had just correctly written. `date_of_birth` is null for anyone who's never gone through the licence-verification wizard — 45 of 81 active SKS staff — so the birthday silently reverted to null on every single save for all of them.
- Fix guards the derive on `date_of_birth IS NOT NULL`. Applied live to ehow via Supabase MCP and verified end-to-end through the real view+trigger path (a real UPDATE persisted correctly, then reverted to null since Aiden Crowley's actual birthday was never confirmed) before the PR landed. Pinpointed via `edge_logs` (exact PATCH timestamps matching `staff.updated_at`) and by listing every trigger on the base table (`pg_trigger`), not by re-reading the already-known `field_people_iud()` again.
- Not resolved here: for a Cards-linked person with BOTH a `date_of_birth` and a differently-valued directly-edited day/month, `date_of_birth` still wins on the next save touching either column — flagged as a product call.

## 2026-08-24 (PR #765 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.548 — Edit Person: job_title dead-write fixed, emergency contact split into 3 structured fields)
*Companion to eq-shell [PR #1567](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-shell/pull/1567) + [PR #1572](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-shell/pull/1572) — see that repo's changelog for the full field_people_iud() collision story (this repo's PR #761 push silently dropped by eq-shell's 0270/0271, found and fixed same session).*
- `job_title` was a real column, sent by this exact form since it existed, but silently dropped by `field_people_iud()` on every save — confirmed live (a real edit left `staff.job_title` at `''`). Not an eq-field code bug on its own; this PR is what actually lets a typed job title survive a save once eq-shell's `0274` (view + trigger) is live.
- Emergency Contact was one free-text input ("Name and phone number"). jvkn's `public.workers` and ehow's own `app_data.staff` already carry it as three structured columns, already kept in sync from Shell-side edits — Field's screen was the one surface still on the combined shape. Split into Emergency Contact Name / Mobile / Relationship; all three now included in the save payload.
- Caught by this repo's own `cache-buster-drift` CI gate before merge, not self-caught: bumped `people.js`'s content without bumping `app-state.js`'s own separate static `?v=` tag in `index.html` (`people.js` is lazy-loaded and picks up `APP_VERSION` automatically; `app-state.js` is eager-loaded and needs its own tag moved every time). Fixed before merge.
- Merged before eq-shell's `0274` was dispatched, on Royce's explicit call — accepted a brief window where every Edit Person save would fail (the payload always includes the two new field keys, which the view didn't have columns for yet), closed by dispatching immediately after merge rather than holding this PR back.
- Not click-tested live by a person — needs an authenticated SKS Core session. Tracked in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

## 2026-08-23 (PR #760 MERGED v3.5.547 — Roster/Edit Roster silently dropped an unrecognized group)
- `getRosterPeopleForGroup()` and Edit Roster's inline equivalent both iterated a hardcoded 4-value group list (Direct/Apprentice/Labour Hire/Subcontractor) with no catch-all, so anyone whose `group` value didn't exactly match one of the 4 was invisible on Roster, the mobile roster view, and Edit Roster — regardless of `on_roster`.
- Contacts already closed this exact class of gap (v3.5.253/255, after an unrecognized `employment_type` silently dropped 2 real people there); the roster surfaces never got the same safety net. Found tracing `on_roster`'s wiring end to end at Royce's request. Verified live first: no SKS person is currently affected — all 71 on-roster staff match one of the 4 — so this is a dormant trap, not a live break.
- New `getRosterPeopleForOther()` surfaces anyone outside the known 4 into an "Other" bucket on all three surfaces, each showing the raw unrecognized value as a diagnosable chip (same pattern Contacts uses). Only appears in the unfiltered view. Also refactored the 3 duplicated inline filter copies into shared `_rosterVisibilityGuard()`/`_rosterApplySearchSiteSort()` helpers.
- 30/30 tests, eslint 0 errors, cache-buster + bundle-drift guards green. Confirmed live via exact `commit_ref` match on the production deploy.

## 2026-08-23 (correction — v3.5.546 was NOT the fix for the reported Contacts problem)
- Royce reported Rhys Scott missing from Contacts. v3.5.546 (below) shipped believing it was the cause. **It was not, and did not fix it.** The real behaviour is deliberate: `renderContacts()` reads `_peopleExMgrs()` (`scripts/utils.js`), which subtracts everyone in `STATE.managers` (`app_data.field_managers` = staff `WHERE is_supervisor`). Its own header says so — *"Contacts minus supervisors/managers (QA row 11, v3.5.228)"*. All 20 SKS supervisors are excluded by design.
- Roster is NOT affected (`getRosterPeopleForGroup()` filters `STATE.people` directly); Royce confirmed live "Rhys is in the roster", so no change was made. Full write-up + the open Shell-vs-Field disagreement in `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.
- v3.5.546 remains a genuine independent bug fix on its own merits — see below.

## 2026-08-23 (PR #759 MERGED v3.5.546 — team-pill filter didn't account for supervisors)
- `personInActiveTeam()` (`scripts/teams.js`), the predicate behind the Contacts/Roster/Timesheets team-pill filter, only ever checked `app_data.team_members`. A supervisor tracked solely via `app_data.team_supervisors` — "who runs a crew" is a deliberately separate concept from "who's in it" — had no `team_members` row, so pilling any specific team silently dropped them from all three screens.
- Found live investigating a real report: Rhys Scott (Site Supervisor) missing from Contacts while a team-member colleague showed fine. Root-caused to a `localStorage`-persisted team-pill filter, not data or RLS — confirmed Rhys's own record satisfies every column the Contacts view filters on, and that the one RESTRICTIVE policy on `app_data.staff` is requester-role-scoped, not row-scoped, so it couldn't explain the difference between two people.
- `personInActiveTeam()` now also passes a person through when they supervise a selected team (new `_teamSupervisorIds()` helper, same per-team caching shape as the existing `_peopleInTeam()`); the "Unassigned" pill now excludes supervisors too, not just members. Doesn't change Rhys's own visibility under today's specific pill — he supervises zero teams too — but closes the general gap.
- `node --check` clean, full `tests/*.test.js` suite 30/30, `eslint@9` 0 new errors/warnings, cache-buster + bundle-drift guards both green. Not click-tested live (Teams is SKS-only, gated behind Core auth).

## 2026-08-23 (PR #758 MERGED v3.5.545 — boot fetch retry/timeout hardening) + PR #756 (leave visibility fail-open closed)
- Built while investigating Royce's "field isn't loading / could not connect to this workspace" report: `loadTenantConfig()`'s three boot fetches (canonical organisations, module_entitlements, tenant-config routing) each had a single hard timeout with zero retry. Added `_fetchWithRetry`/`_fetchWithTimeout` — org fetch retries at [3000,6000]ms, entitlements at [1500,3000]ms, routing at [4000,7000]ms, each reporting to Sentry (`EQ_OBS.captureException`) on final failure rather than failing silently.
- This was NOT the actual cause of the reported outage — the real cause was a dropped anon grant on jvkn's `public.organisations` (see `eq/pending/eq-shell.md`, 2026-08-23), found via a live deploy-preview smoke test after this fix's own CI passed. Kept as independent hardening rather than discarded, and said so plainly rather than letting the wrong diagnosis stand.

## 2026-08-23 (direct migration apply — no PR — ehow RLS: 26 tables closed for hardcoded-org_id-only policies)
- Two waves, same session: 11 tables (`audit_log`, `competencies`, `people_notes`, `supervisor_notes` + 7 more sharing the same shape) then a suite-wide re-check found 15 more (`app_config`, `organisations` — both anon-reachable — plus 12 more authenticated-cross-tenant tables including `tenders`, `site_audits`, `nominations`). All had a policy qual gated only on a hardcoded SKS `org_id`, with no check on the caller's own JWT `tenant_id` — any authenticated user from any org could reach the 12 (`app_config`/`organisations` were worse: reachable with no login at all).
- Fix: AND the caller's JWT `app_metadata.tenant_id` claim onto the existing qual on all 51 policies across the 26 tables — no grant changes, no schema changes. For `app_config`/`organisations` this closes anon specifically (anon carries no JWT, so the added clause never matches) while leaving authenticated SKS users unaffected.
- `supabase/migrations/20260823_audit_apprentice_tables_jwt_tenant_gate.sql`, `20260823_apprentice_tables_jwt_tenant_gate_inert.sql`, `20260823_ehow_second_wave_jwt_tenant_gate.sql` — applied directly against ehow (`ehowgjardagevnrluult`) via Supabase MCP, not through this repo's normal CI/PR pipeline (no dispatch runner covers ad-hoc RLS-only migrations the way the tenant-fleet migrator does). Each migration verified its own result live via an internal `DO $verify$` assertion before returning success.
- Not yet click-tested by a real SKS login — see `ops/pending.md` (2026-08-23) for the open item.

## 2026-08-23 (PR #755 MERGED — Dashboard licence-missing card: one row per person, not per licence)
- `renderLicenceExpiryAlert()` (`dashboard.js`) rendered one row per (person, licence) alert, so a person short 2+ credentials repeated their name once per credential — Royce's screenshot showed "Aiden Crowley" twice, once for White Card and once for Photo ID. Grouped by person: one row per person, all their missing/expired/expiring issues stacked as separate badges underneath. Existing missing-first/soonest-first sort preserved for free — grouping by first occurrence in the already-sorted alerts list keeps a person's row at their single most-urgent issue's position, no re-sort needed.
- `getLicenceExpiryAlerts()` (`people.js`) untouched — grouping is render-layer only, and it has exactly one consumer. Header count badge still counts issues, not people, by design (not part of the ask).
- Verified via direct function-level browser testing: this sandbox has no network path to reach eq-canonical (reproduced identically on both localhost and the real deploy preview URL — confirmed a Browser-pane tool limitation, not a CSP or app bug), so injected synthetic `STATE.people` data and called the real edited function directly. Confirmed one row per person, correct badge count per person, correct sort with mixed missing+expired issues, correct per-issue action buttons.
- CI's cache-buster drift check caught 2 missed `?v=` tag bumps (`app-state.js`, `core-bundle-b1.js`) on the first push — bumping `APP_VERSION` doesn't move each script's own independent `?v=` tag in `index.html`. Third time this specific miss has recurred this month; local memory note strengthened to run `node scripts/check-cache-busters.mjs --base origin/main` before every future version-bump push instead of relying on CI to catch it.
- eq-field [PR #755](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/755), fully green CI, squash-merged on explicit "merge." Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`'s `CACHE` literal reading `v3.5.544`.

## 2026-08-21 (PR #752 MERGED — Roster compliance gate, part 2: worker-facing compliance card on My Schedule)
- Companion to PR #750 (same day, below) — that badge is supervisor-only. Royce's own framing: "the whole model for me is based around empowering and encouraging our employees to ensure their profile is correct... across apprentices, labour hire and direct employees... higher compliance with less input from the business." My Schedule — the page a worker opens about themselves, not a crew-wide view — gets a status card: green "up to date" when compliant, amber naming the specific missing credential when not. Always renders both states, not just the gap.
- Real scope correction found before writing code: assumed My Schedule needed a fix to default to the logged-in worker's own name. It already does — `index.html`'s `refreshPersonSelects()` reads `sessionStorage.eq_logged_in_name` with fuzzy-match fallback, and has locked the picker to self-only for apprentices since v3.5.172. No identity-resolution code needed; the actual gap was narrower than scoped.
- Reuses `_missingRequiredTypes()` unchanged via a new `_myComplianceCard()` (`people-required-credentials.js`), deliberately different tone from the supervisor badge. Uses the existing `.eqf-mcard--ok`/`--warn` status-card primitive rather than ad-hoc colors.
- No EQ Cards deep link — no Cards URL exists anywhere in this codebase (checked, not assumed). Card names Cards as the destination; a real link is a fast follow once that URL is confirmed.
- Labour Hire's shared per-agency login (`auth-agency-gate.js` — a business account, `agencyMode`/`agencyName`, no individual identity) means this feature and #750's badge both can't reach that group until they're individually linked, same identity primitive (`EQ_PERMS.myPersonId()`) both rely on. An agency-level aggregate nudge was scoped as a real option and explicitly deferred, Royce's call.
- Verification: same battery as #750 (bundle/cache-buster checks, eslint 0 errors, full 30-file test suite) plus live-browser verification against the real shipped files with synthetic data — confirmed auto-select, the amber gap card, and the green compliant card all render correctly. No live click-through by a person yet, same limitation #734/#737/#750 already logged.

## 2026-08-21 (PR #750 MERGED — Roster compliance gate: missing-required badge on both roster views + an assignment hold point)
- The missing-required-credential badge (PR #734, merged 2026-08-19) only ever rendered on Contacts; its Dashboard companion (PR #737, same day) didn't reach the roster views either. Nobody editing the actual roster looks at either of those pages. Live-verified via Supabase MCP before building anything: 27 of 65 linked SKS workers are missing Photo ID and/or White Card (the only two org-wide requirements configured), 11 of those actively rostered onto real sites in one sampled week — 43 person-days across Telstra – St Leonards, St George Private Hospital, Arncliffe – Ultegra, and others.
- `_missingRequiredBadge()` now also renders on Weekly Roster (desktop grid + mobile day strip) and Edit Roster — same function, same guarded loader-kick pattern `dashboard.js`'s alert card already used safely.
- New hold point: assigning a real site (not a leave/education code) to a flagged person pops a confirm naming the specific gap and requires a typed reason before the save proceeds. Doesn't block the assignment — Field can't edit canonical licence data (the gap can be "not uploaded yet," not just "doesn't hold it"), and an urgent job still needs covering — but the override is now logged to `audit_log` (category `Compliance`) instead of slipping through unseen. Royce's explicit choice (`AskUserQuestion`, then re-confirmed via a `/decide` pass) over a hard block, a badge-only no-op, and a split-by-credential-risk option. New `_rosterGateAssignment()` (`people-required-credentials.js`) is the single choke point both save paths (`roster.js`'s `updateCell()`, `roster-cell-picker.js`'s `_rv9QuickEdit()`) now funnel through; reuses the existing `#modal-confirm` dialog (`teams.js`'s `_teamsConfirm` Promise pattern) plus an additive `#confirm-reason` field the ~6 other callers of that modal never touch.
- Also fixes Sentry EQ-FIELD-17 (live, unresolved): `people.js`'s `renderContacts()` called `_ensureRequirementsForList()` unguarded — a cross-file reference that could throw `ReferenceError` if it raced ahead of `people-required-credentials.js`'s lazy-chunk load. Guarded the same way `dashboard.js` already was. Marked resolved in Sentry post-deploy.
- Version-number collision on merge: PR #749 landed v3.5.540 to main while this PR was in flight, also claiming v3.5.540. Rebased, resolved the `index.html` changelog-banner conflict (kept both entries, renumbered this one to v3.5.541), re-ran the full verification battery (bundle check, cache-buster check, eslint, all 30 test files), force-pushed, re-confirmed CI green, merged. eq-field [PR #750](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/750) (v3.5.541), squash-merged, confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js`.
- Not yet click-tested live by a person — same sandbox limitation #734/#737 already logged (no Core-authenticated SKS session reachable from this environment). Verified instead via the full test/lint/bundle battery plus live-browser verification against the real shipped files with synthetic `STATE`/canonical data, driving the actual DOM through the full confirm/cancel/empty-reason/audit-write flow.

## 2026-08-20 (PR #748 MERGED — Documents to Sign: fixed the real sign-write bug, header-merge order)
- `ehowFetch()`'s header merge (`Object.assign({headers: merged}, init)`) put `init` as the last source in a shallow merge; `sign`'s PATCH call is the only call site that passes its own `init.headers` (`Prefer: return=minimal`), which wholesale-overwrote the entire merged headers object — `apikey`/`Authorization`/`Content-Profile` included. PostgREST rejected every Sign attempt: `401 "No API key found in request"`.
- Confirmed via Netlify's own function logs from Royce's live test, not inferred — grants and triggers on `document_signoffs` re-checked clean first.
- Live since v3.5.534 (#743) — every sign attempt across this entire thread (v3.5.534-538) was doomed regardless of which View-side fix was live, since none of them touched the write path.
- Fix: compute the merged headers object last so it always wins. eq-field [PR #748](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/748) (v3.5.539), merged, confirmed live, confirmed working by Royce (his test document shows SIGNED, cross-verified in Shell's admin register).

## 2026-08-20 (PR #747 MERGED — Documents to Sign: Office viewer got stuck on mobile, dropped it)
- The `isOffice` routing (v3.5.535) sent `.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.pptx` through `view.officeapps.live.com`, which Royce reported getting stuck on mobile. Removed entirely — View now always opens the raw signed URL and lets the browser/OS handle it natively, matching Royce's original working desktop behaviour before any routing was added. eq-field [PR #747](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/747) (v3.5.538), merged, live.

## 2026-08-20 (PR #746 MERGED — Documents to Sign: "Couldn't sign" now shows the real reason)
- Six materially different server responses on the sign path all collapsed into the same hardcoded toast, even though the real error text was already being extracted into `e.message` two lines away. Now shown when present. Observability fix only, not a root-cause fix — but it's what caught #748's bug on the next real attempt. eq-field [PR #746](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/746) (v3.5.537), merged, live.

## 2026-08-20 (PR #744/#745 MERGED — Documents to Sign: View button popup-block, then iPhone Safari blank tab)
- Desktop Chrome: `window.open()` after two awaited network round-trips was being silently blocked. Iterated to opening a blank tab synchronously first, then redirecting once the URL resolved (v3.5.535, [PR #744](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/744)).
- iPhone Safari: that same redirect-after-await trick doesn't work on WebKit — a tab opened, never navigated. Replaced with a genuine second-tap "Open document" button using an already-resolved URL, so the click itself is the direct synchronous `window.open()` on every browser (v3.5.536, [PR #745](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/745)).
- Both merged, live.

## 2026-08-20 (PR #743 MERGED — Documents to Sign: found the real root cause, four rounds in)
- `document-signoffs.js`'s `ehowFetch()` never sent `Accept-Profile`/`Content-Profile: app_data`. `document_register`/`document_signoffs`/`document_versions` all live in `app_data`, not `public` — every call (`list`/`view`/`sign`) had been 404ing upstream since this endpoint was first written, surfaced as a generic "Upstream query failed"/"Write failed" the whole time.
- Two rounds of production `DROP`+`CREATE VIEW` and a Supabase project restart, chasing a schema-cache-staleness theory, were both harmless but never the fix — a `curl` without the profile header (matching what the code actually sent) would have ruled that theory out immediately.
- eq-field [PR #743](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/743) (v3.5.534), merged, live.

## 2026-08-20 (v3.5.530-533 MERGED — Documents to Sign: pilot opened up, nav-visibility gate fixed twice, sidebar Logout hidden when Shell-embedded)
- Pilot access removed entirely — any signer with ≥1 outstanding row sees the nav item now, not just two hardcoded emails. eq-field [PR #739](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/739) (v3.5.530).
- Nav-visibility gate fixed twice: first, an async token-timing race was latching a permanent false instead of a retryable null (v3.5.531); then, the gate was only ever invoked from `showPage()`, which never runs for the default desktop-manager dashboard landing — added an unconditional call in `initApp()` (v3.5.532).
- Unrelated small fix mid-thread: `logoutUser()` never touched Shell's session cookie, so the sidebar Logout button was a no-op when Shell-embedded — now hidden in that mode only (v3.5.533).
- All merged, live.

## 2026-08-19 (PR #732 APPLIED — security: revoke anon SELECT on app_config.value, live PIN/token leak)
- `public.app_config` (ehow) granted `anon` — unauthenticated — full-column SELECT, including `value`. That table stores the SKS `staff_code`/`supervisor_code` PINs and a TAFE API token in plaintext. Confirmed live with the project's own anon key: `GET ?select=key,value` returned 200 with the real values. No credential of any kind required — worse than the same-day role-blind-but-authenticated write gaps.
- Fix is column-level, not a blanket anon revoke: verified `checkSupabaseHealth()` depends on anon-readable `key`, and the only anon consumer of `value` is a dead pre-login codes fetch (both live tenants are Core-only). Revoked anon's table-level SELECT, re-granted `id`/`key`/`org_id` only. `authenticated` untouched.
- Hit a real gotcha applying it: a column-level `REVOKE` alone is a no-op against an existing table-level grant — had to revoke the table-level grant first, then re-grant the safe columns.
- Applied live to ehow via Supabase MCP (Royce's explicit go), smoke-tested live pre/post with the anon key. eq-field [PR #732](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/732).

## 2026-08-19 (PR #728 MERGED + APPLIED — RLS: manager/supervisor-only write on 6 site-report tables)
- `prestarts`/`toolbox_talks`/`incidents`/`site_diaries`/`site_audits`/`site_audit_items` enforced tenant/org scoping only on write, no role check — any authenticated SKS session (incl. apprentice/labour_hire) could INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE any of these tables directly via PostgREST, bypassing `permission-matrix.js`'s manager/supervisor-only create+submit gate.
- Added RESTRICTIVE INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies requiring `app_metadata.eq_role IN ('manager','supervisor')` on all 6 tables. Read is unaffected. Verified against real client write paths first — no independent employee/apprentice write path exists for any of them.
- eq-field [PR #728](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/728), squash-merged. Migration hand-applied to ehow via Supabase MCP (Royce's explicit go), post-apply verified.
- **Substrate correction surfaced in the process**: eq-shell's `tenant-migrate.yml` ("the One Pipe") only reads migration files from its own repo — it never sees eq-field's own `supabase/migrations/*.sql`. Every prior migration in this lineage claiming "dispatches via the One Pipe" has actually gone in by hand via Supabase MCP. See `sessions/2026-08-19.md` for detail.

## 2026-08-18 (PR #725 MERGED — v3.5.522, boot-perf: digest-settings.js moved off the critical path)
- First of the 2026-07-28 audit's candidates — measured EQ Field's real boot performance first (the audit note was 3 weeks stale by the time this ran) before moving anything. Moved to lazy-load on the Managers page.
- Caught a real bug in the process: digest-settings.js self-registered via `document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', ...)`, which would silently never fire once the script loads after the page has already parsed (exactly what lazy-loading does) — fixed with a `document.readyState` check that runs immediately if the event has already passed.
