## 2026-07-31
- **PR #583 MERGED (v3.5.390)** — fixed the Supervision page's "📧 Weekly supervisor digest" opt-in panel, which had silently stopped rendering for everyone. Reported by Royce ("I thought it was under supervisors"). Root cause: `scripts/digest-settings.js` (untouched since v3.4.59) triggered the panel by monkey-patching `window.renderManagers`, polled for 5s at `DOMContentLoaded` then permanently abandoned — but `scripts/managers.js` (which defines `renderManagers`) became lazy-loaded in v3.5.21 (`EQ_LAZY`), usually not fetched within that window, so the wrap never installed. Replaced with a `MutationObserver` on `#page-managers`'s `class` attribute — `showPage()` removes `hidden` synchronously, before the lazy bundle even starts fetching, independent of load timing. The panel is a DOM sibling of `#managers-content`, not a child, so it was never actually at risk from `renderManagers()` re-renders. No DB change — `field_managers.digest_opt_in` and the Friday send (`supabase/functions/supervisor-digest`) were unaffected throughout. Verified via an isolated harness reproducing the exact race (old code fails it, new code passes) since live tenant data isn't reachable from the build sandbox; confirmed live post-deploy on `field.eq.solutions` (`sw.js` banner) and via a demo-tenant click-through. Real-SKS-data click-through still needs Royce (`sks/pending.md`).

Leading theory by end of session: Chrome partitions a third-party iframe's service worker/cache separately from a direct top-level visit to the same origin, so nothing done on a standalone `field.eq.solutions` tab was ever going to reach the Shell-embedded copy Core actually loads. Retired `_DASH_MAP_CORE_VERTICAL_SHIFT` entirely (a fraction-of-canvas-height heuristic that had to be re-guessed every round, since the busiest cluster's own bounding-box spread changes week to week) in favour of an explicit anchor: pans vertically only (horizontal framing from `fitBounds()` untouched) so a fixed Sydney CBD reference point (`-33.8688, 151.2093`) always lands dead-centre, regardless of which sites are in the busiest cluster that week. - **Stale-version-footer pattern — resolved, no code change.** Open the entire session (reported 4+ times, surviving both #633's more-visible update banner and a Chrome-storage-partitioning theory/fix attempt). Root cause: browsers only check a Service Worker for updates on an actual page load/navigation — a long-lived SPA tab that's never actually reloaded never re-checks, no matter how many deploys land. Fix was procedural, not code: hard-refresh the *actual* tab being tested in (not a fresh standalone tab), click "Reload now" if the update banner appears. Confirmed working ("445 showing").

Added `.btn.edit-only`, which as a side effect also closes the identical latent gap on 8 pre-existing `.btn.edit-only` buttons that were never actually hidden despite already carrying the class: Roster's "Edit Roster" link, Timesheets' Batch Fill/Import CSV/Multi-Week Export, and Job Numbers' CSV/Import/Add Job Number/Show Retired. Version bumped to 3.5.499 (not 3.5.498) — origin/main advanced mid-session when #697 merged while this fix's worktree was being set up; caught before writing any code, rebased onto the new tip first. 27/27 test files pass (run individually — `node tests/*.test.js` silently only executes the first shell-expanded arg), eslint 0 errors, `build-bundles.mjs --check` all 6 green. Confirmed live via Netlify MCP — deploy `6a7eda48` on `field.eq.solutions`, `commit_ref` matches the merge commit `5199b51` exactly, production context, secret scan clean.

Email Templates admin page moved from its previous nav location into the main Manage group. Also fixed an empty "Pipeline" nav heading left behind as a side effect of #702's opt-in change.

The other 28 of 41 real page ids had no guard at all. Replaced all 5 with a single `PAGE_ACCESS` deny-by-default registry covering every page id — an id with no entry is refused, not rendered. Two ids were real `isManager`-only features with no permission-matrix key to map onto (`permission-matrix.js` v2.4): `field.manage_data` (the Data/Import-Export page — exports and `confirmReset()` had **zero** permission check of any kind, worse than the already-guarded pages; new checks added there plus an outright sks-tenant block, matching the nav's existing intent) and `reports.safety_dashboard.view` (Safety Dashboard was the only `reports.*` view page still on raw `isManager`). Edit Roster's guard also moved from raw `isManager` to `canManageRoster()`. Verified: full test suite green (incl. the permission-enforcement-drift guard), eslint 0 errors, `build-bundles.mjs --check`/`check-cache-busters.mjs` both clean, `PAGE_ACCESS` logic directly exercised via the live deploy preview and (post-merge) production as an unauthenticated session across tenant/permission combinations.
- **PR [#708](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/708) MERGED (v3.5.505) — `apprentices.js`'s profile drill-down had no view-level identity gate.** Found in the same audit: `renderApprenticeProfile()` had no check on whose profile was being opened — any authenticated role could view any apprentice's full profile (skills, ratings, feedback, journal, quarterly reviews), not just their own. The list view is deliberately open for self-service and every mutation already checked `canManageApprentices()`; the drill-down itself had nothing. Fix allows viewing when the profile belongs to the signed-in person or `canManageApprentices()` is true, else the standard empty state. Identity resolution exposes `EQ_PERMS.myPersonId()` (was private to `permissions.js`, already used internally by `getCrewPersonIds()`/`crewFilterFragment()`) rather than a second parallel lookup. Also folded the same check into `isSelfView` (unshared journal-entry visibility), previously `staffTsPerson`-only — the legacy standalone-PIN login path, missing the Shell/Core-authenticated path that's the common case on both live tenants today. **Rebase note**: branched before #707 merged; #707 landed first and touched the same version-bump lines + changelog insertion point, so this PR needed a manual rebase onto the new `main` before merging — `core-bundle-a2.js` regenerated fresh from source after resolving rather than trusting the raw git auto-merge. Verified live in a browser console with seeded `STATE`/`apprenticeProfiles` across 5 scenarios (logged out, own profile, someone else's profile, manager viewing either), reading the actual rendered DOM each time.

- **PR [#805](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/805) MERGED (v3.5.582) — Edit Roster: fix Ctrl+Enter not filling on the first press.** Royce reported two things from an Edit Roster screenshot: the Fill Week button "looks like it saves but then this happens" (many rows showing empty Tue-Fri), and Ctrl+Enter "kind of working" — jumps to the next row but only fills if you click back and press it again. Root-caused the keyboard bug: `updateCell()` (`scripts/roster.js`) always commits through `_rosterGateAssignment()` (`people-required-credentials.js`), which is unconditionally async — even the "gate is off" case re-checks a feature-toggle cache via `.then()`. The keydown handler (`scripts/roster-editor-keyboard.js`) called `fillWeek()` synchronously right after `updateCell(el)`, so it read `entry.mon` before the just-typed value had actually landed in `STATE.schedule` — first press filled nothing (or a stale value) while focus still jumped away regardless, so it looked like it worked; a second press on the same row then read the by-then-committed value and filled correctly. Fixed by sequencing `fillWeek()` + the focus jump inside `updateCell()`'s now-returned commit promise.
  - **Investigated the Fill Week "reverts" report per Royce's explicit ask to dig into the mouse `⇒wk` button path too, before accepting "it's just the keyboard."** That button calls `fillWeek()` directly — no `updateCell()`/async-gate involved, so the race above can't touch it. Checked the exact people/week from the screenshot directly against live `ehow` (`app_data.schedule_entries` + `audit_log`): every row had the correct Mon-Fri data. The audit trail showed a real ~5-minute Ctrl+Enter burst (11:23-11:28 UTC) lining up almost exactly with the screenshot's own "Updated 21:23" (AEST) stamp — the screenshot most likely caught the grid mid-traversal, not an actual revert. No data-loss bug found or fixed on the mouse path. Two real but out-of-scope gaps surfaced in the same investigation (SKS canonical roster rows never get a real `id`/`updated_at` from reads; realtime doesn't adapt narrow `schedule_entries` records, so cross-supervisor live sync is inert for SKS) — flagged, not fixed; see `eq/pending/eq-field.md`'s 2026-08-26 entry.
  - **Two false leads caught and retracted mid-investigation, before being reported as fact**: a query joining `schedule_entries` against `audit_log` for corroborating timestamps produced a 2-3x row-count fan-out that briefly looked like real duplicate data — a direct `GROUP BY staff_id, date HAVING count(*) > 1` against the table alone disproved it. A "Nabeel Hussain" name-mismatch theory (roster display name vs. his actual `app_data.staff` record, filed as "Mohammed Hussain") looked like a silent write failure — his actual `schedule_entries` rows checked out correct.

## 2026-08-27 (no PR — live config fix — digest test-send's third bug, found the morning after #802 shipped)
- Royce tried "Send test to myself" after #802 (v3.5.578) went live and hit "Test send failed: unauthorized" again — same symptom, new cause. `DIGEST_CRON_SECRET`, the corrected credential #802 introduced, had silently failed to save to Netlify: a `manage-env-vars` MCP tool call had reported "Environment variable upserted," but a follow-up `getEnvVar` read came back "Not Found." The tool's own success message was wrong.
- Set the var directly via the raw Netlify API (`createEnvVars`) instead, confirmed it actually persisted this time, then triggered a fresh production build (`createSiteBuild`) — env vars are baked into a function's runtime config at build time, so an already-running function doesn't pick up a change without a new deploy, confirmed empirically (the first rebuild, triggered before the var write was confirmed real, picked up nothing).
- Verified live via a credential-free probe against the real endpoint: response changed from `500 {"error":"Server missing AUDIT_SB_URL/AUDIT_SB_KEY/DIGEST_CRON_SECRET"}` to `401 {"error":"Not authenticated — please log in again."}` — proof the function now reaches the real session check instead of failing on missing config. Real click-through by a logged-in manager/supervisor is still the one remaining verification step (see `eq/pending/eq-field.md`).
- Also merged two unrelated in-flight PRs on Royce's explicit request (neither authored in this conversation — both from separate concurrent sessions, each carries its own full writeup above): [#805](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/805) (v3.5.582, Ctrl+Enter roster fill race) and [#810](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/810) (v3.5.580, observability.js DOMException handling, closes EQ-FIELD-18). Both confirmed live via direct commit-ancestry checks against the newest ready production deploy at merge time, not just a green `gh pr merge`.

## 2026-08-27 (field_people SEC-33 visibility regression — part 1, DB-only)
- `app_data.field_people` (security_invoker=on) inherited eq-shell's SEC-33 RESTRICTIVE own-row-or-manager read policy on `app_data.staff` (migration 0256, applied 2026-08-21 to ehow+zaap) — correct on the base table, since it protects DOB/emergency-contact/pay-rate PII. But `STATE.people` is core boot data read by 42 files (roster, timesheets, leave, safety, dashboard, calendar...), not just `roster.js`, so 88 of 110 SKS staff — everyone who isn't a manager/supervisor — has seen at most themselves, tenant-wide, since that date. Same root cause as `field_managers`'s identical collapse (see the entry directly above/eq-field PR #813), but not fixed the same mechanical way here, since `field_people`'s column list genuinely carries PII the restriction was right to protect.
- Fix: new `app_data.field_people_directory` view, `security_invoker=false` with its own inline `tenant_id` filter — same exception shape as `field_managers`. Exposes only a Royce-approved coworker-safe subset (name/first/last/preferred name, group, trade, licence, agency, hire_company, job_title, on_roster/archived/active/field_approved, start_date, user_id, worker_id, timestamps). Excludes DOB (all 3 forms), email, phone, emergency contact (all 3 forms), PIN, rating, TAFE fields (`tafe_day`/`year_level`/`rto`) — those stay own-row-or-manager-only on `field_people`. `field_people` itself unchanged.
- Applied live to both `ehowgjardagevnrluult` and `zaapmfdkgedqupfjtchl` via Supabase MCP ahead of the PR (this repo's standing hand-apply convention — no CI apply path for `supabase/migrations/*`). eq-field [PR #814](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/814), squash-merged on explicit "merge", CI green.
- Verified via live JWT simulation, real non-supervisor SKS worker, separate transaction from the apply: `field_people` stayed at 1 row for that worker (unaffected); `field_people_directory` returned all 74 active/approved people (was 1); a bogus `tenant_id` claim returned 0 (cross-tenant isolation intact); querying `dob` against the new view errors `42703` (column does not exist) — structurally absent, not filtered.
- **No client-side changes in this PR — nothing reads the new view yet.** Part 2 (repointing `roster.js` and the other `STATE.people` consumers, without breaking a worker's own profile-edit screens) is separate, deliberately deferred follow-up work — see `eq/pending/eq-field.md`.

Since managers keep the full `field_people` view under this design, none of the 5 are reachable by a non-manager — zero data-corruption risk.

## 2026-08-30 (Leave requests from non-manager accounts could silently save with no approver linked)
- Royce tested a leave request from a dummy apprentice account ("Jordan A. Sample"), picked Royce as approver, got a confusing "no email on file for approver" toast. Hunch was apprentice access restrictions — right in spirit, wrong mechanism.
- Root cause, verified live via simulated-JWT SQL against ehow: `loadCanonicalStaffMap()` (scripts/supabase.js) resolved approver/requester names to `staff_id` via a raw, unrouted `/rest/v1/staff` fetch, bypassing `sbFetch`'s own role-based routing. A manager JWT gets full-tenant SELECT; a non-manager gets zero rows. Any approver/coworker name then failed to resolve — `leave_requests.approver_id` saved null, invisible to that supervisor's own approval queue, surfacing only as the misleading email toast several steps downstream. Confirmed on one real prior submission too (Collin Toohey, 2026-07-04), not just today's test.
- Third independent discovery of this exact bug class in 4 days (a security_invoker=on view/table silently collapsing to 0 rows for non-managers) — see the 2026-08-27 `field_managers` dropdown-population fix and the `field_people` SEC-33 fix. This one took a different shape: rerouted through `sbFetch('people', ...)`, reusing the already-shipped `field_people_directory` coworker-safe view (SEC-33, PR #814) instead of flipping another view's RLS as a 4th one-off exception.
- Also added the same pre-submit resolution guard for the approver that already existed for the requester — an unresolvable name now blocks submission with an honest message instead of saving silently linkless. Royce's explicit choice after seeing the tradeoff stated.
- eq-field [PR #844](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/844) (v3.5.615), squash-merged on explicit "yes", confirmed live.

## 2026-08-31 (v3.5.621 — PR #853 MERGED + LIVE — Teams: untick-to-remove silently didn't save)
- Collin Toohey reported: adding people to a team via Manage Teams saves, but unticking someone to remove them doesn't. Root cause: `_teamsManagerBodyHtml()` (`scripts/teams.js`) renders the edit panel into two permanent containers — the modal (`#manage-teams-body`) and the standalone Teams page (`#teams-summary-list`) — that mirror each other; `openModal`/`closeModal` only toggle a CSS class and never clear content, so once a team had been edited once, both held a live duplicate of the same checkboxes (one visible, one hidden). `saveEditTeam()` read them via an unscoped `document.querySelectorAll`, which matched both copies — a person counted as "keep" if *either* copy was checked, so adding always worked (both copies start unchecked) but removing never did (the hidden copy stayed checked, since the user never touched it).
- **Fix**: new `_teamsEditorRoot()` helper resolves whichever container is actually on screen (mirrors the existing modal-vs-page detection `startEditTeam()` already used for scroll-into-view); `saveEditTeam()` and `createTeamFromForm()` now scope every form-control read to it. `createTeamFromForm()` had the identical duplicate-ID bug on its name/colour inputs — fixed alongside, same root cause.
- Verified against the real, unmodified `scripts/teams.js` via a local static-server harness (`npx serve` — raw `file://` silently fails to resolve the app's relative `<script src>` tags, discovered mid-session) exercising `startEditTeam`/`saveEditTeam`/`createTeamFromForm` against a DOM shaped like the two real containers. 10/10 checks passed: remove-via-modal, remove-via-inline-page, add-still-works regression, create-team fix.
- CI caught a real cache-buster drift on first push (`app-state.js`'s static `?v=` tag in `index.html` wasn't bumped alongside `APP_VERSION` — `teams.js` itself is lazy-loaded so needed no tag). Fixed before merge.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge it", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.621`).
- Not click-tested live by a real SKS supervisor through Core — no SKS/Core credentials in this environment. This bug has likely been live since the standalone Teams page shipped (v3.5.256) — worth a live roster spot-check for any team member who was supposedly removed in the past and it silently didn't take.

A `/decide` pass upgraded the first-draft design (a read-only mirror list) to removable chips — a chip is both the summary and its own remove control, so there's one source of truth instead of two lists to reconcile by eye.
- Page-only (`scripts/teams.js renderTeamsPage()`) — the Manage Teams modal opened from Roster/Contacts/Schedule/Timesheets is untouched. Page widens 640→960px only while a team is being edited, collapses back on Cancel/Save. New `_renderSelectedSummary()`/`_removeSelectedTeamMember()`; new `id="edit-team-members-list"` on the existing checkbox wrapper for a single delegated `change` listener rather than one per checkbox.
- The summary panel does NOT use `_teamsEditorRoot()` (the modal-vs-page helper `saveEditTeam()` uses) — it's pinned unconditionally to the page's own checkbox list, since it only ever renders there. Caught during design review: without this, the panel could silently show the modal's stale tick-state if the modal happened to be left open from an earlier navigation. Covered by its own test scenario.

- CI caught a cache-buster drift on first push — `app-state.js`'s tag again, same shape as the v3.5.621 catch. Fixed before merge.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge it", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.623`).
- Not click-tested live on SKS — no Core/SKS credentials in this environment, same disclosed gap as v3.5.621.

- The whole row (colour swatch, name, member count) now opens edit via `startEditTeam()`, matching the audit log's existing `.audit-row-jumpable` hover/click pattern (new `.team-row-clickable` in `styles/base.css`). `role="button"`/`tabindex="0"`/Enter-Space keyboard handling included, not just a mouse-only `onclick`.

## 2026-09-01 (v3.5.626 — PR #858 MERGED + LIVE — roster/timesheets group classification silently null for managers + people_removed)
- Asked to investigate a bug PR #855 flagged but didn't fix (3 call sites selecting `group` literally, assumed to 400 for managers on `field_people`). Live verification against ehow found the premise stale: eq-shell's own migration `0249_field_people_view_parity.sql` (applied to ehow 2026-08-19, untracked in eq-field's own migration ledger) already added a real `group` column to `field_people`/`field_people_removed`, alongside a hardcoded `NULL` `employment_type` stub. The 3 flagged call sites need no fix — `group` already resolves everywhere.
- The real, bigger bug was in the same function: PR #855's `_jwtPhysical()` fix (v3.5.625) only translates `employment_type`→`group` on the non-manager/directory-routed branch. A manager's own roster/timesheets staff-name-map fetch, and any read of `people_removed` (never directory-routed), still asked for `employment_type` and silently got `null` instead of the real classification — every manager, since 2026-08-19.
- Fixed by widening the translation to the whole `people`/`people_own`/`people_removed` family regardless of routing branch, superseding PR #855's narrower version. No call-site changes needed. 7 new/changed `_jwtPhysical` test cases (45/45 total), `roster-adapter`/`timesheets-adapter` suites unaffected.
- Caught and fixed two secondary CI gaps while verifying: `app-state.js`'s own cache-buster tag (same recurring drift class), and a `max-lines` breach on `scripts/supabase.js` (1551 vs the 1550 grandfathered ceiling) — trimmed the comment rather than raising the ratchet.
- Rebased once over concurrent PR #857 before merge; squash-merged on explicit "merge #858", confirmed live via the actual Netlify deploy record (`commit_ref` match, `context: production`, `state: ready`) rather than the merge event alone.
- Not click-tested live on SKS as a manager — no Core/SKS credentials in this environment. Spawned a follow-up background task for the recurring root cause (3rd incident of eq-shell's pipeline silently changing a shared `app_data` object) — closed same day, see `sessions/2026-09-01.md`.

## 2026-09-01 (v3.5.630 — PR #864 MERGED + LIVE — Weekly Roster site dropdown could stick on "All Sites")

- Closes the follow-up v3.5.629 flagged but didn't fix (spawned `task_028d9925`): the read-only Weekly Roster page's `#roster-site` shares Edit Roster's exact `#editor-site` population code — `refreshPersonSelects()` populates it from `getAllSiteCodes()` (lazy-loaded `roster.js`) once at boot, before `roster.js` has necessarily loaded, and nothing re-triggers it once the tab is shown. Confirmed reachable, not just theoretical — traced the full call graph (`refreshData()`'s 30s/5min poll never calls `refreshPersonSelects()`; `showPage('roster')` only calls `renderCurrentPage()`, never repopulates the select) before touching anything.
- **Fix differs from v3.5.629's**, deliberately: `#roster-site` is the older, established pattern Editor's filter was modeled after, with 3 read consumers vs Editor's 1, so dropping it would be a bigger UX change than removing something that had never worked since it shipped hours earlier. Kept the dropdown; self-healed the race instead — `renderRoster()` (scripts/roster.js) now re-runs `refreshPersonSelects()` once if the select is still stuck on "All Sites" by the time it can run (roster.js is guaranteed loaded by then), then no-ops on every render after.
- Reproduced live in a real browser tab before fixing: a temporary harness (deleted before commit, never part of the diff) fetch+eval'd the real shipped `refreshPersonSelects()` out of the real `index.html` and loaded the real `roster.js` against fixture data — confirmed the bug precondition, confirmed the fix heals it to the real site list, confirmed idempotent on repeat renders. Full existing roster test suite green (51+131+18 tests, `roster-rules`/`roster-adapter`/`roster-cell-picker`), lint/syntax/bundle/cache-buster checks all clean.
- Attempted a live click-through smoke test on the deploy preview — hit the same documented Core-only sign-in gate as production for the `eq` tenant (expected, matches existing docs, not a bug). No standalone credentials in this environment for either tenant, same standing limitation as most security-gated PRs in this changelog.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge it", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.630`).

## 2026-09-01 (PR #859 + #865 + #866 MERGED, migration confirmed LIVE — tenant_role_overrides role-default bypass fixed)
- **Corrects the 2026-08-31 entry above**: that entry's "Ships `supabase/migrations/20260831_field_permission_denials_enforce.sql` — NOT APPLIED, Royce's dispatch" is stale — live `pg_get_functiondef` confirms it was applied at some point after, with no PR recording it.
- **The bug**: every one of the 10 functions + 3 (`schedule_entries`) RLS policies that migration touched grants access via `role_default OR (extra_perms ? key AND NOT denied_perms ? key)` — the deny-check only ever gated the extra_perms path. A caller who already qualifies via the bare role-default branch (manager, or manager/supervisor) never reaches the deny-check at all, so denying a Field-domain key for a manager/supervisor via Shell's Access Control silently did nothing on any of these 13 objects.
- **Not universal**: of the 6 RLS policies originally suspected, only the 3 `schedule_entries` ones shared the bug. `labour_hire_companies_select`/`labour_hire_rates_select` already wrapped the deny-check around both disjuncts correctly (origin unexplained — see `eq/pending/eq-field.md`); `staff_conversations_creator_and_perm` has no role-default branch to bypass.
- **Fix**: `supabase/migrations/20260901_field_permission_denials_role_bypass_fix.sql` restructures all 13 to `(role_default OR extra_perms ? key) AND NOT denied_perms ? key` — deny wins regardless of which path would otherwise grant it. eq-field [PR #859](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/859), merged. CI failed once on `migration-shared-fn-guard.test.js` (the migration never cited `IDENTITY-MODEL.md` §3.3.3 for the 7 shared-registry functions) — fixed, comment-only.
- **Merged with the migration deliberately un-applied**, held for Royce's go. On that explicit go-ahead, re-ran the migration's own pre-apply gate live (all 13 objects still matched the assumed OLD shape, zero drift) and applied it (ledger `20260901024203`); a second, independent session checked at almost the same moment, found it already live with no visible attribution yet, and opened eq-field [PR #865](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/865) to correct the header — not an unaccountable hand-apply, just two sessions racing to close the same gap. eq-field [PR #866](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/866) hit a real rebase conflict against #865 and merged both sessions' verification into one accurate header, which is what's actually live on the migration file today.
- **Functionally verified**, not just textually diffed: evaluated `schedule_entries_insert`'s actual live `with_check` clause against simulated JWT claims (pure expression evaluation, no writes) — a denied manager now correctly blocks; a plain manager, and an employee holding the grant with no denial, are both unaffected.
- `shell_control.tenant_role_overrides` (jvkn) checked twice, before and after: zero rows target any of the 7 affected keys either time — no real SKS user's access has changed; this is a correctness fix ahead of demand.
- **Also found and fixed along the way**: the day-old `.githooks/pre-push` cache-buster check false-positived on any Windows checkout (`core.autocrlf=true` — `readFileSync`'s CRLF-converted bytes vs `git show`'s LF-normalized ones, compared as if equivalent). eq-field [PR #861](https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/861), merged, confirmed working on a later push through the same hook.
- **Also found, not fixed here**: eq-shell's CLAUDE.md under-describes `eq_enforce_function_privacy` for ehow — says `public` schema only and a blind revoke-with-no-restore; live it also covers `app_data`+`service` and auto-restores prior grants. Flagged as its own eq-shell task, in progress as of this close.

## 2026-09-01 (v3.5.625 — PR #855 MERGED + LIVE — roster/timesheets staff-name map 400ing for every non-manager)
- Royce reported Edit Roster going blank after a refresh, attaching his own browser console log — traced to one 400 rather than guessed at: `GET field_people_directory?select=...,employment_type` → `42703 column does not exist`.
- Root cause: `_jwtPhysical()` (`scripts/supabase.js`) routes a non-manager's (supervisors included — confirmed via Royce's own log, he's a supervisor) GET of `people` to `field_people_directory` for the roster/timesheets staff-name map. That view renames `employment_type` to `"group"` — the select fragment still asked for `employment_type`, 400ing the whole request and collapsing every person to "(unknown)" for the whole tenant. Live-broken since v3.5.615 (2026-08-30), ~2 days, hit both roster and timesheets (same staff map feeds both adapters).
- Fixed by translating `employment_type`→`group` in the query string, scoped to the branch that's actually routing to the directory view — the manager path (`field_people`) was untouched. Added 4 test cases; the branch that broke had zero prior coverage (none of the 3 pre-existing `_jwtPhysical` tests ever set `isManager`).
- Verified live beyond the test suite: JWT-simulated SQL against ehow (real supervisor `app_metadata` claims) confirmed the corrected query returns real names + group values.
- **Two live version collisions during merge** — PR #854 and PR #856 both landed as v3.5.623/624 while this was in flight. Rebased twice, renumbered to v3.5.625 both times, full test/lint/bundle/cache-buster re-run after each. Second rebase caught a real miss: `app-state.js`'s own `?v=` tag in `index.html` hadn't moved (auto-merged to an identical literal on both sides, so it never showed as a conflict or a diff) — caught by the automated checker before push, not after.
- **Own mistake, caught before it mattered**: an early `git rebase` targeted the shared `C:\Projects\eq-field` root instead of an isolated worktree (copy-paste error out of the `/brief` skill template), silently rebasing whatever branch was checked out there. Assessed before doing anything further: that branch (`claude/csv-import-preserve-existing-fields`) was already fully merged upstream weeks earlier with its remote already deleted — zero unique commits, nothing lost. Redid the actual rebase correctly from the real worktree afterward.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge", confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.625`) and the deployed `scripts/supabase.js` source directly.
- Not click-tested live on SKS — no Core/SKS credentials in this environment, same standing gap as most entries in this file.

- Follow-up to PR #858 (above): the schema change that made #858's wider fix necessary — eq-shell's own migration adding a real `group` column to `field_people`/`field_people_removed` on ehow, 2026-08-19 — was untracked anywhere in this repo. Ran via `/decide` (full six-step reasoning in `docs/reflection-log.md`): write a documentation-only migration file rather than leave it flagged-only, since the failure mode it closes (a future session trusting a stale local file over live state) had already recurred once the same day, on this session's own PR #855.

## 2026-09-01 (PR #870 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.632 — FIX: safety forms "Pull from roster" could fail with a misleading date error)
- Royce reported directly: "pulling from roster for the safety forms (prestarts, toolboxes etc) doesnt work - it says something about could not resolve from todays date."
- Root cause: `getWeekForDate()` is defined only in `leave.js`, which the `site-reports`/`prestart`/`toolbox`/`diary`/`safety` lazy-load tab buckets (`lazy-loader.js` TAB_SCRIPTS) never load. A session opening Prestart/Toolbox/Site Diary without Leave already visited that session hit the `typeof getWeekForDate === 'function'` guard as false and got "Could not resolve roster day from date" — blaming the date when the real gap was a missing script. Same latent-lazy-dependency class this file has hit and fixed repeatedly (tafe.js/jobnumbers.js/teams.js gaps, `openLeaveRequestSafe`, `_leaveAction`).
- Fixed all 4 affected call sites (`site-reports.js` ×2, `toolbox.js`, `diary.js`) — each now self-heals via `EQ_LAZY.loadTabScripts('leave')` before computing the week/day key.
- Verified: full unit-test loop + `eslint@9` (0 errors) + bundle-drift + cache-buster checks all clean (the cache-buster check caught a real miss on first run — `app-state.js`'s own `?v=` tag hadn't moved — fixed before push). Live browser verification reproduced the exact bug precondition (`getWeekForDate` undefined after loading only the `toolbox`/`prestart` buckets) and confirmed the real roster-pull functions self-heal correctly from that state; 2 of the 4 call sites verified this way directly, the other 2 by identical-pattern code review only (disclosed, not overclaimed).
- Not click-tested through the full authenticated UI — no Core/SKS session in this environment; the deploy preview's own login redirects to "Sign in through Core" for any code path. Flagged as an open follow-up rather than claimed.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge" (`8126888a`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.632`).

So the exact artifact Royce described ("field says 1st year") was never directly observed in the DB this session; the fix is grounded in the code path (the hardcoded 1, confirmed by direct read of `apprentice-write.js`), not in finding Jordan's own bad row — disclosed as such rather than overclaimed.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #880 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.641 — FIX: My Schedule compliance card mislabeled a viewed teammate's gap as "Your profile")
- Royce shared two My Schedule screenshots (himself vs. Harry Barton selected in the picker), asking whether "Your profile needs Photo id" showing while Harry was selected meant the gap was his own or Harry's.
- Root cause: the My Schedule name picker isn't restricted to your own name (`refreshPersonSelects()` only locks it for apprentices) — a supervisor routinely lands on someone else's schedule — but `_myComplianceCard()` (`scripts/people-required-credentials.js`) hardcoded "Your profile" regardless of who was selected.
- Fix: the card now compares the selected person against the logged-in session name (same fuzzy match `refreshPersonSelects()` already uses) and names them explicitly ("`<Name>`'s profile needs...") when it isn't the viewer. No session name known defaults to the prior "Your" framing. `_recheckMyCompliance()` untouched.
- Version collision mid-flight: two other PRs (#879, #878) merged to `main` while this was open, both already claiming v3.5.639/640. Rebased, renumbered to v3.5.641, re-ran the full verification suite, force-pushed.
- Verified via an isolated Node `vm` harness (real source, stubbed globals) covering all self×other × compliant×missing combinations, reproducing the exact reported bug and confirming the fix. Full test suite (36/36), lint (0 errors), bundle-drift, and cache-buster checks all clean. Attempted a live click-through against the real Netlify deploy preview itself (not just the local sandbox) — confirmed both `eq` and `sks` are Core-only even there, so no environment reaches My Schedule without a real Core sign-in; flagged as an open follow-up rather than claimed.
- **Separate finding surfaced during the rebase, not fixed here**: an already-merged eq-field commit (`0e7d3956`, PR #878, v3.5.640) contains a fabricated incident in both its commit message and its own `docs/reflection-log.md` entry — a specific, detailed "prior compliance-card fix + version collision" narrative that never happened. No such commit exists anywhere in `git log`, and the code that entry claims was already fixed was still the unfixed original until this PR. Told Royce directly in-session; triage tracked in `ops/pending.md`.
  - **Correction (2026-09-02, same-day follow-up, PR #883): the bullet above is wrong.** #878 never touched `people-required-credentials.js` (`git show 0e7d3956 --stat` — only `roster.js`), so the check that produced it was against the wrong file. #878's account is genuine: its "compliance-card fix, two commits before this one" is PR #877, and #877's own real merged diff contains exactly the reflection-log corruption #878 describes fixing (a PR #875 bullet duplicated into the wrong section). Full detail in eq-field's `docs/reflection-log.md`, PR #883's entry.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #884 MERGED + LIVE — e2e: allowlist Netlify's edge bot-challenge 403 in shell-mode leave test)
- Handed a report that `tests/e2e/leave.spec.js` under the `shell-mode` Playwright project failed against a real deploy preview with `console.error: ... 403`, never locally, only under shell-mode — asking whether it's a real latent bug or an intentional block the test doesn't yet allow for.
- Root-caused via the actual failing GH Actions run's own trace.zip (run 33574666983, target `deploy-preview-883`) rather than re-running blind: in both the original attempt and its retry (140 captured requests each), the only non-200 is the page's own first `GET /?tenant=demo` → 403, immediately followed by `POST /.netlify/submit-challenge` → 200 — Netlify's own edge bot-challenge (`sec-auth.netlify.com`, `__nfsec` cookie) reacting to the HeadlessChrome UA, self-healing within ~1s. Confirmed `eq_shell_mode` sessionStorage only toggles a CSS class plus a staff-auto-mode branch that never engages for this test's mocked-supervisor login — not app code, not `netlify.toml`/`_headers`/CSP, not shell-mode-specific behavior. It landed on this test only by being the 9th/10th full-page navigation in that job; 8 prior navigations in the same run were clean.
- Fix: extended `ALLOWED_CONSOLE_PATTERNS` in `tests/e2e/_helpers.js` to tolerate this known-benign, self-healing platform noise, documented at the same rigor as the file's existing entry — deliberately broad (the browser's console text carries no URL to key on more precisely), a named, accepted tradeoff rather than an oversight.
- Verified live, not just theorized: re-ran the real `e2e-smoke.yml` workflow against this PR's own deploy preview (`deploy-preview-884`, run 33576147584) — 9/9 passed, including `[shell-mode] leave.spec.js`.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge" (`4b3086cd`), confirmed live via Netlify's own deploy record for that exact commit (`published_at` set, `state: ready`, 13s build — no bundler in this repo), not just merge success. No version bump — test-only change, nothing shipped to `field.eq.solutions` changed.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #885, v3.5.645, MERGED + LIVE — sync_degraded: surface crew-table failures alongside people)
- A PostHog review of today's traffic surfaced `sync_degraded` (Field's own core-data health check) firing 12 times for 7 real SKS users, with the `people` table implicated in every single one — either alone or as part of a full 5-table failure. Investigated whether this pointed at a slow query, a missing index, or too-tight a timeout: all three ruled out by reading the actual fetch code and the live view/RLS definitions — no client-side timeout exists on this path at all, and the RLS policies are flat JWT-claim checks with proper indexes.
- Real finding: `people` only *looked* like it failed alone because of wave-timing, not a `people`-specific fault. `loadFromSupabase()` fires `people` + 3 crew tables (teams/team_members/team_supervisors) as "Wave 1," then 4 other core tables as "Wave 2" only after Wave 1 resolves — and Wave 1's crew-table fetches were wrapped in a blanket catch that never reported failures anywhere. A blip landing only in Wave 1 showed as "people failed"; one spanning both waves showed as the full 5/5.
- Fix: `_loadCrewTables()`/`_emitSyncHealth()` now surface crew-table failures too (`crew_failed` in the `sync_degraded` payload), closing the blind spot. Purely additive — no change to the toast copy, the dedup signature, or resilience behavior.
- Could not pull live Supabase query logs for the actual incident window — the Supabase MCP server failed to connect this session (`CONNECT_TIMEOUT`). Read of the pattern (12 events/7 users over 13 hours, not clustered) is that this is ordinary transient connectivity from field workers on mobile connections, which is exactly the scenario this health check was built to survive gracefully — not evidence of a performance problem needing a fix.
- Tests + lint clean. Squash-merged on explicit "merge" (`386be805`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (v3.5.645).

## 2026-09-02 (PR #890, v3.5.650, BUILT + CI-GREEN — NOT MERGED, Royce's deliberate call)
- Follow-up audit (Royce: "run the audit") for the same "repeating action, no de-dupe, no report" bug shape that caused the eq-shell alias-spam bug (see eq-shell changelog, same date) found a more severe instance in this repo: `copyLastWeek()` (`scripts/batch.js`) wraps each per-person schedule save in its own `try/catch` that swallows the failure, so `Promise.all(savePromises)` could never see a rejection — the "some saves failed" toast was dead code, and a failed copy always reported "Copied N cells… saved ✓" even if every write failed. The false success was masked further because `STATE.schedule` was already updated in memory for the instant local render, so nothing looked wrong until the next reload silently reverted it.
- Confirmed the fix pattern against a live, correct sibling in the same file: `applyBatch()` (Batch Fill) has no inner catch and already surfaces real failures correctly. Removed the two inner catches in `copyLastWeek()` to match.
- Before merging, checked real usage in `public.audit_log` on ehow rather than assume: 2 uses ever (2026-07-19, 2026-08-25 — the second a 172-cell blanket fill), each by a different person. Presented this to Royce alongside the fix. His call: leave the fix built and tested, but **do not merge** — a rarely-used bulk-copy feature that fills cells without a fresh per-person look is in tension with his own empty-cell-is-bright-yellow design (a filled cell reads as "handled" whether a human decided it or automation copied a stale value forward); making it safer risked making it more attractive to reach for, which isn't the goal. See `eq/pending/eq-field.md` for the standing "not merged, Royce's call" tracking.
- Tests + lint clean; hit and resolved a same-day version collision with PR #889 (renumbered 649→650) before pushing. PR https://github.com/eq-solutions/eq-field/pull/890 open, not merged.

## 2026-09-02 (PR #896, v3.5.653, MERGED + LIVE — Weekly Roster: the roster table is the landing view again)
- Same conversation as PR #890 above. Royce reviewed a screenshot of Weekly Roster and called the summary card at the top (coverage %, not-rostered chips, tap-a-site crew list) "useless, hard to manoevre... annoys me everytime i click here." First explored redesigning the card's *content* (week-wide gaps across all 7 days instead of one, quick-filter buttons, new-starters, management-out promoted up) and ran a feasibility check on all 4 candidates — then Royce pulled back the whole direction: "i feel we have complicated this - i want someone that clicks on weekly roster to see the weekly roster."
- Re-read `renderRoster()` with that framing and found the actual bug: the card rendered unconditionally above the roster table on every render, completely independent of the existing By Crew/Grid toggle beneath it — there was never a way to see the roster without scrolling past the card first. Fix: stop calling `_renderRosterOverviewCard()` from `renderRoster()`. The card's computation is untouched in `scripts/roster-overview.js` in case a lighter, on-demand version is wanted later.
- Real tradeoff, not a pure win, and named as such before building: the card also carried "Management Out This Week" and the not-rostered gap chips, neither of which has a replacement on this page now. Royce chose speed-to-table over that visibility, explicitly, after seeing the tradeoff.
- Hit and resolved a same-day version collision with PR #894 (renumbered 652→653), including a real (non-conflicting) auto-merge alongside #894's own unrelated `fillWeek()` change in the same file — verified by re-reading both edited spans post-rebase, not just trusting a clean auto-merge. Tests + lint clean (0 errors; a small pre-existing warning-count drift from other same-day merges, unrelated to this diff, confirmed by line-location). `gh pr merge --delete-branch` failed locally on the same worktree-collision error as earlier PRs today — merge had already succeeded via the API, cleaned up the remote branch by hand. Confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (v3.5.653) and, afterward, by Royce clicking through Weekly Roster on Core himself: "confirmed, table loads first now."

## 2026-09-02 (PR #893 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.654 — FIX: Roster/Editor/Schedule could flash "wiped" while a week's data was still loading)
- Royce, live on Core+SKS Edit Roster: "we filled this in this morning - where did it go" — then, moments later on a different week, the exact same thing, with his own instinct already correct: "I think this happened previously... is it a connectivity thing?"
- Root cause: `STATE.schedule` only holds a ±1-week sliding window, and week navigation (`roster-week-nav.js`'s `slideEditorWeek`/`slideRosterWeek`/`slideScheduleWeek`) re-rendered `renderEditor()`/`renderRoster()`/`renderSchedule()` immediately with no check that the new week's data had actually finished loading. A week still in flight read as "genuinely empty," which Edit Roster painted as a loud amber "needs filling in" cell with the day name as its `<input>` placeholder — visually indistinguishable from the roster having been wiped — until the real values landed a moment later and silently overwrote the placeholders. `_preloadAdjacentWeeks()`, the function whose entire job was preventing exactly this, was a literal no-op stub with a comment asserting an assumption ("data is already loaded globally") that was never actually guaranteed.
- Fix: replaced the stub with `_ensureSurfaceWeekLoaded()`, mirroring the render-now/background-load/redraw-on-arrival pattern `_ensureApprenticeWidgetWeeksLoaded` already uses elsewhere in this codebase — render immediately, kick a background fetch if the week isn't resident, redraw once it lands. Wired into all three affected render functions so the "empty/needs attention" flag is gated on the week actually being loaded, not just currently empty. Uses a 15s retry cooldown (not the apprentice-widget's permanent give-up-after-one-failure latch) specifically because Edit Roster uniquely disables the app's own 30s background poll — a permanent latch would have left a supervisor stuck on a genuinely-failed week with no automatic recovery, caught during a reflection pass before this shipped.
- Live evidence, not just code reasoning: pulled two real Sentry issues explaining why the underlying fetch is sometimes slow/absent on exactly this Shell-embedded SKS path — `EQ-FIELD-19` (AbortError on the tenant-config routing fetch, since 2026-08-27) and `EQ-FIELD-16` (Failed to fetch inside the sign-in token mint, since 2026-08-18) — both pre-dating this fix, so not caused by any recent deploy. Not fixed here (a deeper, separate issue); spun off as its own background task, which Royce started running in a separate local session.
- Added 4 real functional tests to `tests/week-window-narrowing.test.js` (resident-week short-circuit, missing-week fetch+redraw, concurrent-caller dedupe, failed-fetch cooldown-with-fresh-state-still-winning) — extracted the real function and ran it against mocked state, not just checking the right identifiers appear in source. Verified: full suite (41/41 test files after this addition, 0 failures), `eslint@9` (0 new errors), `build-bundles.mjs --check` and `check-cache-busters.mjs` both clean.
- Opened the actual PR #893 deploy preview and confirmed no console errors on load, then specifically tried the demo-tenant PIN gate to test for a click-testable path around the standing Core-only sandbox limitation — it also dead-ends at "Sign in through Core," the same finding this repo's history already recorded 3+ times before, now re-confirmed a 4th time rather than assumed from memory.
- **Version collided three times in a row while this PR was open** — PR #892 (Apprentices), then PR #894 (Edit Roster fill-week fix), then PR #896 (Weekly Roster overview-card removal) each independently claimed this PR's version number in turn, all four sessions shipping roster-adjacent fixes within roughly the same 30-45 minute window. Rebased and renumbered v3.5.651→652→653→654, re-running the full verification pass after each hop before force-pushing. No actual code ever conflicted across any of the four collisions — only the shared `index.html` changelog-banner block and `docs/reflection-log.md`'s tail section needed manual merging each time; `scripts/roster.js` itself auto-merged clean against both #894's and #896's edits, confirmed by re-reading the merged result rather than trusting a clean auto-merge alone.
- Squash-merged on explicit "go ahead and merge it" (`cff4f25b`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.654`). `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`'s local branch-delete step failed (a different concurrent worktree had `main` checked out) even though the squash-merge itself succeeded server-side — confirmed via the GitHub API directly rather than trusting the error, then deleted the remote branch manually.

## 2026-09-04 (PR #905 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.663 — FIX: Timesheets Unlock Week confirm() dialog never reached the screen through Core)
- Spun off from PR #904 (Prestart/Toolbox Reopen fix, same day) — that PR's own commit found and correctly flagged `unlockCurrentWeek()` as the same bug class in a different feature, deliberately left out of scope. This session picked up that follow-up.
- `unlockCurrentWeek()` (`scripts/timesheets.js`) called raw `window.confirm()` despite the same file already having its own `_tsConfirm()` modal wrapper (v3.5.33, BUG-009-safe, already used 4x elsewhere for fill/copy/repeat). Root cause independently re-verified against eq-shell's live `FieldIframe.tsx` this session, not just trusted from PR #904's comment: `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-scripts allow-forms allow-downloads allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox"`, `allow-modals` genuinely absent — per the HTML spec, `confirm()` silently returns `false` with no dialog inside that sandbox. SKS is Core-only, so Unlock Week had done nothing at all, silently, for every real supervisor for as long as the button has existed under Core.
- Fix: `unlockCurrentWeek()` now awaits `_tsConfirm('Unlock Week', <same message text>, 'Unlock')` instead of calling `window.confirm()` directly — mechanical swap, same message, same pattern as the file's own `fillTsWeekFromMon`/`fillTsWeekDNW`/`repeatTsDay` call sites.
- Found two more live instances while there, same file: `lockCurrentWeek()` and `requestTsUnlock()`, both still raw `window.prompt()`. Needed a text-input modal, a different shape of fix — spun off as a separate task (`task_26ee3a6f`) rather than folded in, matching PR #904's own precedent for this session's bug.
- Verified: full test suite (40/40) + eslint (0 errors, 938 pre-existing warnings, none new) + build-bundles (6/6 match — `timesheets.js` confirmed lazy-loaded, not a bundle source) + cache-buster check all clean (caught and fixed one real miss: `app-state.js`'s hardcoded tag in `index.html` doesn't auto-bump the way lazy-loaded files do). Not click-tested live — standing Core-only limitation; also independently re-confirmed this session that a plain local static server can't substitute either (`tenant-config` Netlify Function 404s with no functions runtime under `npx serve`).
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge" (`c5c3d7bd`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.663`).

## 2026-09-04 (PR #908 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.666 — FIX: Timesheets Lock Week silently locked with no reason, and Request Unlock was a dead button, through Core)
- Companion to PR #905 above — closes the last 2 live BUG-009-family instances in `scripts/timesheets.js` found while fixing Unlock Week, spun off as `task_26ee3a6f` and started by Royce in a separate local session.
- `lockCurrentWeek()`: reason is optional, and the existing `if (reason === null) return;` already handled a real Cancel correctly — but through Core the `window.prompt()` never rendered at all, so the week locked with `reason: null` every time regardless of what the supervisor meant to type. A silent wrong default, not a no-op.
- `requestTsUnlock()`: no permission gate by design (any signed-in worker can ask, not just supervisors) — a blocked `prompt()` returns `null`, identical to the function's own Cancel check, so the button did nothing at all with zero feedback, indistinguishable from broken.
- Fix: new `_tsPrompt()` helper in `timesheets.js`, sibling to the existing `_tsConfirm()` — same shared `#modal-confirm` dialog, same Promise/fallback shape, reusing the dialog's existing `#confirm-reason` textarea (already had a real precedent consumer, `people-required-credentials.js`'s `_rosterConfirmGap`) so no new `index.html` markup was needed. Reason stays optional, matching the original `window.prompt()` behaviour exactly. `requestTsUnlock()` became `async` (was sync) — confirmed safe, both call sites are inline `onclick` handlers, same pattern as the already-async `unlockCurrentWeek()`. `eslint.config.js`'s `max-lines` ratchet for `timesheets.js` bumped 2450→2500, matching the `apprentices.js` v3.5.555 precedent for the same situation. Grepped the sibling `timesheets-*.js` lazy-load family for any other raw `window.prompt`/`confirm`/`alert` — none found.
- Landed two version-collision rebases while pushing (v3.5.664→665→666) — PRs #906 and #907 each merged out from under this branch; `timesheets.js` auto-merged cleanly both times (different regions of the file), only the `index.html` changelog banner and `docs/reflection-log.md` needed manual conflict resolution.
- Verified: full suite (41/41) + build-bundles (6/6 match) + cache-buster (clean) + eslint (0 errors, 939 pre-existing warnings, none new). Not click-tested live — same standing Core-only limitation.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge" (`90df171b`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.666`), verified by polling rather than assumed instantly live (production still served v3.5.665 for roughly the first stretch after merge).

## 2026-09-04 (PR #903 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.661 — My Schedule: show the job number(s) logged in Timesheets, for Workbench entry)
- Royce, from two screenshots: Cameron Tregoning's My Schedule showed no job number for any day that week, while his Timesheets grid for the same week showed real logged job numbers (28120 on Monday, a split day 28101/28165 on Tuesday). Asked "should Cameron show the job numbers?" first — investigated before assuming a bug: My Schedule's existing job line (`resolveCellJob()`) was always the PLANNED job (a roster pin, or a site linked to a Project with a Job Number), which most sites don't have configured; Timesheets' job number is freely typed at time of logging hours, with `site_id` deliberately left null and no link back to the roster at all (confirmed by reading `timesheets-adapter.js`'s own header comment, then live-verifying against Cameron's actual `schedule_entries`/`timesheets` rows on ehow — schedule carried no job pin, timesheets carried the real 28120/28101/28165).
- Put the finding to Royce via `AskUserQuestion` (leave as-is / pin jobs on the roster / surface the logged job on My Schedule too) — he initially chose "leave as-is," then came back after seeing his own screenshots side by side and asked for exactly the third option: "This screen should be showing the job numbers entered in the timesheet so that employees know what jobs they enter into our payroll system called Workbench."
- Built: new `loggedJobsForDisplay()` (`roster.js`) parses Timesheets' packed per-day job string (`"JOB:hrs|JOB:hrs"`, same grammar `timesheets.js` itself already parses) into a clean, deduped list. `renderSchedule()` now also reads `STATE.timesheets` for the person+week — already resident, since `_loadWeeks` (index.html) fetches `schedule` and `timesheets` together for the same week — no new network call needed. Renders as a new "🧾 For Workbench" line, additive alongside the existing planned-job line; a day with nothing logged shows nothing extra.
- New `tests/schedule-workbench-job.test.js` (6/8 initially, extended to 8 same day — see PR #907 below), extracted and evaluated directly from source (same technique `week-window-narrowing.test.js` uses).
- `eslint.config.js`'s `max-lines` ratchet for `roster.js` bumped 1900→1950 (one 50-increment, matching this file's own established rounding convention) — the addition pushed the file to 1,911 lines.
- Attempted a possible workaround for the standing Core-only click-test limitation: `?tenant=demo` against a local static server (`npx serve`) — still fails, `tenant-config` Netlify Function 404s with no functions runtime under a plain static server. Confirms the existing limitation rather than finding a new way around it.
- Verified: full suite (40/40) + eslint (0 errors) + build-bundles (`roster.js` confirmed not a bundle source) + cache-buster clean.
- Squash-merged on explicit "merge it" (`5d5a027b`), confirmed live via `field.eq.solutions/sw.js` (`v3.5.661`).

## 2026-09-04 (PR #907 MERGED + LIVE, v3.5.665 — My Schedule's "For Workbench" line now shows hours too, not just the job number)
- Same-day follow-up. Royce: "We need to show the hours aswell for each day" — a split day (like Cameron's Tuesday) showed both job codes ("28101 · 28165") but not which hours went to which, so Workbench entry still needed a trip into Timesheets to check.
- `loggedJobsForDisplay()` extended to take the day's hours too: a single bare job reads its hours from the separate day-total field (`<day>_hrs`), while a split day carries each segment's own hours inline after the colon — mirrors `timesheets.js`'s own `rawJob`/`rawHrs` handling exactly (re-verified directly against that file's parse logic before writing this, not assumed from memory). The rare case of the same job typed into two segments sums its hours rather than showing/dropping either one. Line now reads e.g. "🧾 For Workbench: 28101 (4h) · 28165 (4h)".
- `tests/schedule-workbench-job.test.js` extended to 8/8 (single job, half-hour values, split days, same-job-twice-sums, empty/malformed-segment cases).
- Rebased once mid-push: PR #906 (Timesheets duplicate-hours fix) landed on main first and independently claimed this branch's v3.5.663 — renumbered to v3.5.665 (v3.5.664 also taken, by #905). `app-state.js`/`sw.js`/`index.html`'s `?v=` tag all showed genuine merge conflicts this time (not the usual silent-duplicate trap, since #906 had already changed the surrounding banner content) — resolved by hand, re-verified all checks clean before force-pushing.

- Investigated against live ehow data before concluding anything: queried `app_data.timesheets` directly for the specific people who looked blank on David's screen — all fully submitted (Dylan 27590/8h, Jessica 26347×2+SICK×2, Marcus 26347×4+RDO, Terry 26347×5, Tara's split days). Then checked `app_data.team_members`: David is only on "Amazon Syd 53" and "Vans"; every person missing from his view is on "CT Team" or "Equinix" instead — neither of David's teams. Read `permissions.js`'s `getCrewPersonIds()`/`crewFilterFragment()` and `permission-matrix.js`'s own history comments to confirm the mechanism: `field.view_all_crews` was manager-only as of v1.7 (2026-07-22, Royce's own prior call) — a supervisor's Timesheets/Leave reads are filtered to just the crews they run/belong to. Not a bug, not data loss — the access-control model working exactly as designed.
- Put this to Royce via `AskUserQuestion` (leave as-is / add David to specific teams / grant him org-wide visibility) — his answer went further than any option offered: "Yeah its a shit feature, supervisors need to be able to see all employees and filter by teams - our teams change dynamically and its easier if they can see everyone." Implemented as a role-default change, not a per-person grant: `permission-matrix.js` v2.9 adds `field.view_all_crews` to the `supervisor` role array (was manager-only), version/history bumped per this file's own established convention. No other logic touched — `getCrewPersonIds()`/`crewFilterFragment()`/`_seedDefaultTeamFilter()` (`teams.js`) already branch on this exact key for managers; supervisors now take the identical code path.
- `scripts/core-bundle-a1.js` regenerated (`permission-matrix.js` is a bundle source, confirmed via `build-bundles.mjs`'s own MANIFEST before assuming). Two hardcoded `?v=` tags in `index.html` needed bumping this round, not just `app-state.js`'s usual one — `core-bundle-a1.js`'s own `<script>` tag, easy to miss, caught by `check-cache-busters.mjs` rather than missed.
- `tests/crew-scoping.test.js` (~34 existing tests, all pinning "supervisor is scoped") reworked, not weakened: `reset()` now strips the new grant back out of the loaded matrix by default, so the existing tests keep proving the crew-scoping algorithm itself still works correctly for a supervisor who doesn't hold the grant (still reachable via a future per-person Shell override, not hypothetical). 2 new tests added confirming the grant against the real, unmodified matrix. 36/36 passing.
- Rebased once mid-push: PR #909 (TAFE/Leave autofill) landed on main first and independently claimed this branch's v3.5.667 — renumbered to v3.5.668, a real conflict on `index.html`'s banner and `docs/reflection-log.md` (resolved keeping both entries), plus the usual silent-duplicate trap on `app-state.js`/`sw.js`/both hardcoded tags (re-verified by hand, not trusted from the clean rebase).
- Named plainly, not softened, in both the PR body and the reflection-log entry: this is a real privacy widening — every SKS employee's timesheet hours and leave-request details become visible to every supervisor org-wide now, not just their own crew's chain. Royce made the call explicitly aware of the tradeoff.
- Verified: full suite + eslint (0 errors) + build-bundles (`--write` then `--check`) + cache-buster all clean post-rebase. Not click-tested live — standing Core-only limitation; the live-data diagnosis itself was done via direct Supabase queries against ehow, not a UI walkthrough.
